MANUAL FOR HANDLING LEGISLATION AND SUBORDINATE LEGISLATIONS

SATISH WAGHOLE SECRETARY (LEGISLATION) LAW AND JUDICIARY DEPARTMENT, MANTRALAYA, MUMBAI.

2025.

H 2316-1

PREFACE

Law is a set of rules that are made by the State to govern the people covering various aspects of human life. In todays modern world the laws are in the form of Statutes passed by the Legislatures. Legislations are drafted within the framework of constitutional provisions. Drafting of legislation is a skillful task that requires precision, clarity and a deep understanding of legal intricacies. In the State of Maharashtra, the responsibility of drafting of laws is assigned to the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department, where the experienced draftsman do to this crucial endeavour.

  • 2. Having served in the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department since 1994, I have witnessed the complexities and challenges inherent in legislative drafting. Over the past 30 years, I have drafted enormous legislations on complex subjects and faced many difficult situations. Through these experiences, I have realized that drafting of legislation requires precision, foresight and an acute understanding of legal principles, which can be achieved only by actual experience in legislative drafting. Many States in India refer Maharashtra State Legislations while drafting their Legislations because its quality and accuracy in drafting.
  • 3. Realising the absence of a standardized procedure in legislative drafting, I embarked on a journey to compile the knowledge and insights accumulated over the years. This endeavour has led to the creation of the Legislative Drafting Manual, a comprehensive resource guide to junior officers and as a ready reference for the officers involved in the process of drafting legislations and subordinate legislations.
  • 4. The primary objective of this Manual is to provide a structured framework and essential guidance to officers engaged in legislative drafting work in the Law and Judiciary Department and various Departments of the Government of Maharashtra. By offering fundamental principles and basic concepts, this manual will be helpful to enhance the efficiency, quality and accuracy in the legislative drafting. This Manual will be an invaluable source of training, guidance and reference to the officers. The Manual will be useful to officers to bring clarity in drafting, precision and to make laws effective.
  • 5. I was working on this project since 10 years. This Manual is prepared on the basis of actual procedure followed in the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department, Government of Maharashtra. It contains important information which will be useful for drafting of legislation and subordinate legislation more effectively and clearly. This Manual will serve as a repository of vital information essential for drafting legislation and subordinate legislation.

H 2316-1a

  • 6. I hope this Manual will serve its best in guiding the officials of Government for initiating legislative proposals and the officers in the Law and Judiciary Department for drafting legislations and subordinate legislations with precision and the best interest of the Government of Maharashtra and to the society. It will also help officials in decision making and strengthening the Government process.
  • 7. The Legislation Branch is established in the year 1974. Year 2024-25 is celebrating as a Golden Jublee Year of the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department. This is a golden opportunity for me to publish this Manual on this occasion. This Manual is the first Manual in India in the Legislative drafting field.
  • 8. I extend my heartfelt gratitude to all the officers of my Department whose contributions have been instrumental to complete this project.

Mumbai, Dated 1st May 2025.

Satish Waghole Secretary (Legislation) Law and Judiciary Department, Government of Maharashtra.

DECLARATION

I hereby declare that, this Manual is not intended for reproduction or reference in any book, media, or in any other form. Unauthorized printing, sale, or publication of its contents is strictly prohibited. The Manual is designed to make available various aspects required, for legislative drafting, including drafting style, structure of drafts, legal phrases used in drafting, guidelines for legislative drafting and subordinate legislation, guidelines for draftsman, stages of legislation procedure, Bill passing procedure, standard format, important constitutional provisions, legal principles developed by courts, etc., for officers involved in the process of legislative and subordinate legislative drafting in the Government of Maharashtra. It is hereby declared that the Manual does not claim to provide an authoritative interpretation of the procedural rules governing the conduct of Business in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and the Maharashtra Legislative Council. Therefore, the users of this Manual are advised that in all cases of doubt, the publications to which references have been made at appropriate places in this Manual as well as the other instructions on the subject should be referred. The Legislative Drafting poem printed in this Manual revealed the qualities and skill required for legislative drafting. The author of the poem is unknown. Certain contents of this Manual are derived from the following books viz., Legislative Drafting by B.E. Atre, Legislative Drafting-Shaping the Law for the New Millennium by T.K. Viswanathan, Principles of Legislation and Legislative Drafting by N.K. Chakrabarti, Interpretation of Statutes by J.P. Singh, Legislative Drafting by G.C. Thornton and Principles of Administrative Law by M.P. Jain and S.N. Jain. These sources have been instrumental in guiding the Legislations.

Mumbai, Dated 1st May 2025.

Satish Waghole Secretary (Legislation) Law and Judiciary Department, Government of Maharashtra.

INTRODUCTION

The Government Departments implement various Acts related to the subjects allotted to them under the Maharashtra Government Rules of Business. Every Department refers legislative proposals to the Law and Judiciary Department for examining its legal and constitutional feasibility and for drafting of Bills and Ordinances; and Subordinate Legislations for vetting and legal scrutiny. All the Legislative and Subordinate Legislative proposals are examined by the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department.

  • 2. Every legislative proposal undergoes various stages. The officers of the Legislation Branch requires to follow certain procedure before introduction of a Bill in the House and after passing of the Bill by both Houses of the Maharashtra State Legislature. The Administrative Department officers also have important role in processing the Legislative proposals and Subordinate Legislative proposals.
  • 3. Many times, the proposals forwarded by the Administrative Departments to the Legislation Branch are drafted hastelily and are vague. Many legislative proposals are received few days prior to the beginning of the Session of the State Legislature or during Session. In such scenarios, the officers of the Legislation Branch not get sufficient time to examine the proposals thoroughly. If a legislative proposal is properly prepared or if the due procedure is followed, it will save the valuable time of both the Legislation Branch officers and the Mantralaya Department officials. Legislations formulated hastily may result in errors, potentially thwarting the intended objectives and leading to litigation. Once the law is enacted it interpret by various stakeholders, including implementing authorities, legal professionals, courts and citizens. Therefore, the legislative proposals must be thoroughly scrutinized and meticulously drafted to ensure clarity in the provisions.
  • 4. It is, therefore, considered necessary to prepare a Manual outlining detailed procedure and providing crucial information for handling legislation and subordinate legislation work.
  • 5. The aim of this Manual is to explain the procedure to be followed in legislation and subordinate legislation process and to provide relevant information required for legislative drafting. The Manual covers various aspects of legislative drafting, including style of drafting, structure of drafts, legal phrases used in drafting, guidelines for legislative drafting and subordinate legislation, guidelines for draftsman, stages of legislation procedure, procedure for passing of Bills, contents of statutes, important constitutional provisions, legal principles developed by courts role of the Administrative Departments and other information related thereto. The Annexures of the Manual contains the basic required information for drafting and dealing legislation and subordinate legislation work. This Manual is the first Manual in the Legislative drafting field covered imfortant aspects required for

H 2316-3a legislative drafting. The contents of the Annexure will be helpful for drafting of legislations and subordinate legislations.

  • 6. This Manual will serve as a valuable resource for the officers of the Legislation Branch in the Law and Judiciary Department, as well as officials from the Mantralaya Department, Government of Maharashtra. It will provide essential guidance for drafting Bills, Ordinances, rules, formulating legislative policy, and understanding the procedure and stages of legislation. Additionally, it will clarify their roles, duties, and responsibilities in the legislation and subordinate legislation processes. The comprehensive framework of this Manual will facilitate the timely and effective implementation of Government policies.
  • 7. The Law and Judiciary Department has framed the Maharashtra Legislation and Subordinate Legislation Procedure Rules, 2023, by Government Resolution, No. LEG-2023/B-594, dated 6th November 2023. On the basis of the said rules, the Manual is prepared with detail procedure and additional information required for drafting of Bills, Ordinances and subordinate legislations.

Mumbai, Dated 1st May 2025.

Satish Waghole Secretary (Legislation) Law and Judiciary Department, Government of Maharashtra.

LEGISLATIVE DRAFTING Legislative drafting is an art, to say, in short, what we ought to do, and what we ought not.

Law is an ocean vast, Its expanse, not static, is a growing mass. Its three-headed growth leaves reefs and rocks, strewn and spread on its path.

Law is madeto dredge and drive past, to meet new situations and task, to reflect the nation’s moods and thoughts.

A counsel faces many handicaps. Time given is generally too short. Instructions often leave room for talk. Those to guide have not much to say beyond, what is contained in some pages short, what may be technically termedas the Department’s scheme of draft.

When a broken field, it may be easy to draft. When the land is virgin, it may not be an easy task to break the land and plough it fast.

Page after page without a flaw or fault may bring no praise or credit to his lot. A mistake at the end, or anywhere in the draft, may wreck his task, leave an indelible blot. A man or two to draft, and world of legislators, Lawyers, Litigants, Judges, Jurists, what an army ! to look and find fault.

When the very critics take to draft, they falter and flop and end: “It is not my task. This, after all, is a legislative draft.”.

DEFINITIONS

  • 1. Definitions.––In this Manual, unless the context otherwise requires,––
  • (1) “Act” means an Act of the State Legislature or Parliament, as the case may be;
  • (2) “Administrative Department” and “Originating Department” means the Department who administer the Act or who initiate the proposal to enact a new law or to make amendments in the Act;
  • (3) “Article” means an Article of the Constitution of India;
  • (4) “Assembly” means the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly;
  • (5) “Assembly Rules” or “MLA Rules” means the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly Rules;
  • (6) “Bill” means a Bill introduced in the State Legislature or Parliament;
  • (7) “Bye-laws” means the bye-laws made by any statutory authority under the Act;
  • (8) “Circular” means an instrument by which the administrative instructions or directions are issued by the Government or Administrative Department or the Authority;
  • (9) “Council” means the Maharashtra Legislative Council;
  • (10) “Council Rules” or “MLC Rules” means the Maharashtra Legislative Council Rules;
  • (11) “earlier law” means any Law, Ordinance, Order, bye-law, rule or regulation passed or made after the commencement of the Constitution by any Legislature, authority or person having power to make such Law, Ordinance, Order, bye-law, rule or regulation;
  • (12) “existing law” means any Law, Ordinance, Order, bye-law, rule or regulation passed or made before the commencement of the Constitution by any Legislature, authority or person having power to make such Law, Ordinance, Order, bye-law, rule or regulation (Article 366(10) of the Constitution);
  • (13) “expediency” means any legislative proposal forwarded by the Originating Department to the Law and Judiciary Department for examining the legal and constitutional feasibility of the proposal;
  • (14) “Financial Memorandum” means the Financial Memorandum appended to the Bill containing the estimate of recurring and non-recurring expenditure, which would involve after the enactment of the law, from the Consolidated Fund of the State;
  • (15) “General Clauses Act” means the Maharashtra General Clauses Act (I of 1904) and the General Clauses Act, 1897 (10 of 1897) (see Annexure XXI);
  • (16) “Instructions” means the Instructions of the President under the proviso to clause (3) of Article 213 of the Constitution for promulgation of an Ordinance by the Governor;
  • (17) “Legislation” means the legislative proposal of the Administrative Department;
  • (18) “Legislation Branch” means the Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department, which undertakes the scrutiny of the legislative proposals and subordinate legislative proposals forwarded by the Administrative Department/ Originating Department;
  • (19) “L.&J.D.” means the Law and Judiciary Department;
  • (20) “Legislative Policy” means the policy framed by the Administrative Department/ Originating Department in respect of the legislative proposal or subordinate legislation proposal;
  • (21) “Legislative Proposal” means the proposal of the Administrative Department/ Originating Department to enact a law or to make amendments in the Act either by introducing a Bill in the Maharashtra State Legislature or to promulgate an Ordinance by the Governor;
  • (22) “Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation” means the Memorandum appended to the Bill providing the details of powers delegated by the State Legislature to the Government or to the authorities subordinate to the Government, to make rules, issue notifications, orders, etc;
  • (23) “Money Bill” means a Bill containing only provisions dealing with all or any of the matters specified in clauses (a) to (g) of clause (1) of Article 199 of the Constitution of India and certified as such by the Speaker;
  • (24) “Notification” means the notification issued in exercise of the powers conferred under any Act, Ordinance or Rules and which is published in the Official Gazette;
  • (25) “Official Gazette” means the Maharashtra Government Gazette specified in Annexure XVII;
  • (26) “Ordinance” means the Ordinance promulgated by the Governor under Article 213 or by President under Article 123 of the Constitution of India;
  • (27) “prescribed” means prescribed to make rules, bye-laws or regulations;
  • (28) “publication” means publication of Bill, Act or Ordinance, Notification, Order, rules, bye-laws or regulations, etc., in the Official Gazette;
  • (29) “Regulations” means the Regulations made under any Act, Ordinance or Rules by the Subordinate authority or body Subordinate to the Administrative Department;
  • (30) “Repugnancy” means the inconsistency between the laws made by Parliament and laws made by the Legislature of State as provided in Article 254 of the Constitution of India;
  • (31) “Rules of Business” means the Maharashtra Government Rules of Business made by the Governor under Article 166 of the Constitution for the affairs of the State;
  • (32) “Rules” means the rules made in exercise of the powers conferred under any Act or Ordinance;
  • (33) “Section” means a section of the Act or Ordinance;
  • (34) “Statement” means the Statement appended to an Ordinance;
  • (35) “Statement of Objects and Reasons” means the Statement of Objects and Reasons appended to the Bill;
  • (36) “Subordinate Authority” means the authority under the administrative control of the Originating Department;
  • (37) “Subordinate Legislation” means an act of making statutory instruments (it includes rules, notifications, orders, bye-laws, regulations or any instrument) by the Administrative Department/ Originating Department or by a body subordinate to it, by exercising the powers conferred on it by the Act or Rules.

Note.– The purpose of the definitions given in the Manual is to understand the meaning of the terms used in this Manual.

H 2316-4

WORK DONE IN LEGISLATION BRANCH

  • 2. Work done in Legislation Branch.- In the Law and Judiciary Department, in the Legislation Branch, the following work is done.-
  • (I) Legislation Work:-
  • (a) Examine the legislative proposals (Expediency) forwarded by the Administrative Department of Mantralaya to make amendments in the State Acts and to enact new Acts (of which subject matter falling in List II and List III of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution of India) and to make amendment in Central Acts (of which subject matter falling in List III of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution) and give legal feasibility/views/opinion thereon.
  • (b) Examine the Expediency of Non-Official Bills forwarded by the Parliamentary Affairs Department to amend State Acts and Central Acts and give legal feasibility/views/opinion thereon.
  • (c) Drafting and processing of Government Bills forwarded by the Administrative Departments.
  • (d) Drafting and processing of Ordinances forwarded by the Administrative Departments of Mantralaya.
  • (e) Proof/Final reading of draft Bill/Ordinance (English and Marathi).
  • (f) Processing work related to Government Bills and Ordinances.
  • (g) Reading of all Bills introduced in the State Legislature (English and Marathi).
  • (h) Publication of English Bills in the Official Gazette under article 348 (3) of Constitution of India, after introduction in House of the State Legislature.
  • (i) Forwarding Authentic copies of Bills received from the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, passed by both Houses of the Maharashtra State Legislature to Governor/President, for assent.
  • (j) Publication of Acts/Ordinances in the Official Gazette.
  • (k) Reading of all Gazette copies of Acts and Ordinances published in the Official Gazette (English and Marathi).
  • (l) Issue Corrigenda/Errata to Acts and Ordinances, if required.

H 2316-4a

(II) Subordinate Legislation Work:-

  • (a) Scrutiny/vetting of Rules, Notifications, Orders, etc., to be issued by Administrative Departments of Mantralaya under Central and State Acts or Ordinances.
  • (b) To give opinion on proposed Rules, Notifications, Orders, to be issued by Administrative Departments of Mantralaya under Central and State Acts or Ordinances.

(III) Work relating to updation of State Acts:-

  • (a) Preparation and updation of Reprints of State Acts.
  • (b) Proof reading of all Reprints of State Acts and Maharashtra Code, forwarded by the Government Press.
  • (c) Uploading of updated copies of State Acts on website of the Law and Judiciary Department (lj.maharashtra.gov.in) and on the India Code Portal (indiacode.nic.in - Government of India website).

(IV) Republication of Central Acts and Ordinances:-

Work related to republication of Central Acts and Ordinances forwarded by the Government of India in the Maharashtra Government Gazette for general information.

  • (V) Miscellaneous Work:-
  • (a) Supply of List of State Acts and Ordinances to other States every year.
  • (b) Applications under the Right to Information Act, 2005.
  • (c) Applications on Public Grievances Portal (PG Portal), Aple Sarkar, ESamiksha Portal.
  • (d) Correspondence from/with Government of India, other State Governments, Private individuals, etc.

TIME FRAME FOR FORWARDING PROPOSALS TO LEGISLATION BRANCH

  • 3.1. Every Ordinance/Bill from drafting stage to publication of Act of the State Legislature undergoes more than 20 stages (see stages in Chapters IV and V). Sufficient time requires for Legislation Branch officers to finalise the draft and to follow the procedure upto introduction of Bill in the House of the State Legislature. There are more than 30 Departments in Mantralaya, each Department initiate legislative proposals either by way of promulgation of an Ordinance or introduction of Bill in the State Legislature. The Legislation Branch also deals other work other than legislation such as, examine the expediency to amend the State Acts and Central Acts, examine expediency of Non-Official Bills, scrutiny/vetting of Rules, Notifications, Orders, etc., to be issued under Central and State Acts (subordinate legislation) of all Mantralaya Departments, updating Reprints of the Acts, etc.
  • 3.2 The Administrative Department/Originating Department shall follow the minimum time frame given below, for forwarding the Legislative Proposals to the Law and Judiciary Department, so that the officers of Legislation Branch will get sufficient time for examining legal and constitutional feasibility of the proposal, drafting and processing the proposal and work related thereto.

TIME FRAME Category of Bill/Ordinance Number of Clauses Time frame to forward Legislative Proposals to Law and Judiciary Department

  • (1) (2) (3) Small Bill
  • (a) Less than 10 clauses 7 days before the commencement of the Session of the State Legislature.
  • (b) More than 10 clauses but less than 25 clauses. 15 days before the commencement of the Session of the State Legislature.

Small Ordinance Less than 25 clauses 7 days before bringing proposal for Cabinet approval. Medium Bill More than 25 clauses and less than 50 clauses 21 days before the commencement of the Session of the State Legislature.

Medium Ordinance More than 25 clauses and less than 50 clauses 15 days before bringing proposal for Cabinet approval.

clauses the Session of the State Legislature. Comprehensive Ordinance More than 50 clauses 21 days before bringing proposal for Cabinet approval.

Note.– Until the process of pending proposals received before Session is completed, the Legislative Proposals received during the Session will not be considered on priority by the Law and Judiciary Department, for introduction in the same Session.

BILL PROCESSING STAGES

Articles 196 to 212 of the Constitution of India provide the Legislative Procedure.

  • 4. (A) Procedure of making reference of Bill to Legislation Branch and Bill processing stages.- When any legislative proposal of a Bill is initiated by any Administrative Department of Mantralaya, the following procedure is generally followed :––

Initiation of proposal.

  • 4.1. Every proposal of Legislation will be initiated in the Administrative Department to which the subject matter of the Legislation relates. Formulation of Policy/prepar ation of proposal by Administrative Department.
  • 4.1.1. Every Originating Department will formulate the policy before preparing any Legislative Proposal, it includes the purpose of the Legislation, scope of the Legislation, achievements by making Legislation, administrative approval of relevant Departments, financial aspects, etc. Concurrence of other Departments.
  • 4.1.2. When the policy is prepared by the Originating Department, and if that policy affects the policy of other Departments or on administrative grounds, it requires the approval of other Departments, the Originating Department shall take concurrence of those Departments. The Originating Department shall also obtain the opinion/ views of the concerned authorities as it deems necessary, on administrative issues involved in the Legislative Proposal. Approval of Finance Department.
  • 4.1.3. If any financial implications are involved or the expenditure would require from the Consolidated Fund of the State after enactment of law, then the consultation/ approval of the Finance Department is required. In such cases, the Originating Department shall take the approval of the Finance Department before forwarding the proposal to the Law and Judiciary Department. Refer file to L. and J.D.
  • 4.1.4. Every Legislative Proposal before submitting to the Cabinet shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department, for examining the legal and constitutional feasibility of the proposal.
  • (a) New Legislation.- If the Legislative Proposal is to make a new law, the Originating Department shall prepare self-contained Note/detailed background note of the proposal and a tentative draft Bill with Statement of Objects and Reasons in English and shall obtain the remarks of the related Authorities/Departments.
  • (b) Amending Legislation/Amendments in Act.- If the proposal is to amend the existing Act, the Originating Department shall prepare amendments in tabular format and in draft Bill format. The Originating Department shall prepare self-contained Note/detailed background note with reasons/justifications of the proposal/proposed amendments and if necessary obtain the remarks of the related Authorities/Departments. Thereafter the proposal shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department, for examining the legal and constitutional feasibility. Time frame. 4.1.5. The time frame mentioned in Chapter III shall be followed by the Administrative Department while making reference of Legislative Proposals to the Law and Judiciary Department. Examining Expediency and Cabinet Note.
  • 4.2. While examining an expediency of any Legislative Proposal in the Law and Judiciary Department, the following aspects will be examined :––
  • (a) Understand the concepts of Legislative Proposal received from the Originating Department.
  • (b) Examine whether it is necessary to amend the law or enact new law; or the purpose can be achieved by making Subordinate Legislation.
  • (c) Examine whether the Legislative Proposal is within the framework of the constitutional provisions which includes,-
  • (i) whether the State Legislature is competent to enact a law on the subject or competent to amend the Act;
  • (ii) to examine under which entry/entries of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution of India as well as other provisions of the Constitution, the subject matter of the proposed Legislation falls;
  • (iii) whether the provisions of the proposed Legislation are repugnant to any Central Act and necessary to reserve the Bill for the President’s assent under Article 254(2) or any other articles of the Constitution;
  • (iv) in case of promulgation of an Ordinance, whether the Instructions from the President are required under the proviso to Article 213 of the Constitution of India;
  • (v) whether the Bill requires the previous sanction of the President under the proviso to Article 304(b) of the Constitution before introduction of the Bill in the State Legislature;
  • (vi) whether the Bill involves any financial implications which would requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207 of the Constitution;
  • (vii) whether the Bill is a Money Bill or not (Article 199 of Constitution);
  • (viii) whether the Bill requires to reserve for President’s assent under Article 31A of the Constitution;
  • (ix) whether the proposed law is for giving effect to the Directive Principles of State Policy (Chapter IV of Constitution) and requires to reserve for President’s assent under Article 31C of the Constitution;
  • (x) whether the provisions of the Bill are applicable to the Scheduled Areas and the Scheduled Tribes (Fifth Schedule) and are in consonance with the provisions of the Panchayats (Extension to Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996 (PESA Act) (40 of 1996).
  • (d) (i) In case of Principal Legislation, whether the Department has prepared detail Note about the provisions thereof and its policy and a tentative draft with the approval of the Government.
  • (ii) In case of Amending Legislation, whether the Department has prepared detail Note of the amendments with justification therefor and a tentative draft of amendments with the approval of the Government.
  • (e) In case of amending Legislation, whether it is necessary to make consequential amendments in the same Act or other Acts.

H 2316-5

  • (f) Whether the proposed amendments/provisions are contrary to any existing Acts (State Acts or Central Acts) or overlapping to each other.
  • (g) Whether it is necessary to give overriding effect to the amending provisions over the other provisions of the Principal Act or provisions of other Acts.
  • (h) Whether it would be necessary to incorporate, saving clause or validation clause or repeal clause or removal of difficulty clause in the draft.
  • (i) Whether the proposed provision would require to give retrospective effect.
  • (j) Whether the Administrative Department has consulted with other Departments if the Legislative Proposal affects the provisions of Act of other Departments or affects the policy of other Departments.
  • (k) Whether the Administrative Department has taken approval of the Finance Department in cases where the proposal involves financial implications on the Consolidated Fund of the State. Submission of Note.
  • 4.2.1. (1) (a) The proposal of the Department will be examined considering the legal and constitutional aspects specified in para 4.2. At this stage, the Law and Judiciary Department will also advise on the necessity or desirability of such Legislation in the light of the existing laws and also constitutional feasibility thereof. After scrutiny of the Legislative Proposal, a detail Note will be prepared and submitted for approval,-
  • (i) to return the file with preliminary observations or with observations for modifications in the proposal; or
  • (ii) return the file with constitutional and legal feasibility, subject to observations to modify the proposal; or
  • (iii) return the file with constitutional and legal feasibility for Cabinet approval; or
  • (iv) return the file with observations regarding lack of legislative competency of the State Legislature.
  • (b) If the tentative draft is forwarded along with the file, the Law and Judiciary Department may,-
  • (i) give approval to the proposal with constitutional and legal feasibility for Cabinet approval; or
  • (ii) make necessary observations in respect of provisions of the draft Bill and may suggest to modify the amendments or provisions of the Bill; or
  • (iii) if consultation of other Department has not taken, it may advice the Originating Department for consultation with other Departments or concerned authorities, etc. Subject matter of proposal.
  • 4.2.2. (2) The Legislation Branch will examine the proposal and if, the proposal is legally feasible, communicate its views to the concerned Department (with or without observations). The Note will provide,-
  • (a) subject matter of the Bill (entries of List II/III of the VII Schedule to the Constitution);
  • (b) repugnancy to any Central Acts arises or not;
  • (c) Previous Sanction of the President under clause (b) of Article 304 of the Constitution is required or not;
  • (d) Governor’s Recommendation under Article 207 of the Constitution is required or not;
  • (e) if the proposal is legally and constitutionally feasible, may suggest, to move proposal for Cabinet approval. Preparation of draft Bill by Originating Department.
  • 4.2.3. (a) The Originating Department shall comply with the observations/objections raised by the Law and Judiciary Department and shall modify the Legislative Proposal accordingly.
  • (b) (i) Statement of Objects and Reasons shall be appended to every draft Bill. The Originating Department shall prepare the Statement of Objects and Reasons, it will contain in brief the background of existing provisions, reasons of amendments/ enactment of new law and objects of the amendments or salient features of the Bill. If it is a Principal Legislation, it will contain in brief the reasons for enactment of law and salient features thereof.
  • (ii) If the Bill would involve financial implications, the Bill shall be accompanied by a Financial Memorandum providing recurring/non-recurring expenditure with the approval of the Finance Department.

H 2316-5a Cabinet Note. 4.3. (3) If it is decided by the Department to proceed with the Legislative Proposal, the Originating Department shall prepare Cabinet Note and route the Cabinet Note through the Law and Judiciary Department. The Cabinet Note shall-

  • (i) state broad features of the Legislative Proposal bringing out clearly the need, scope and object of the proposed Legislation;
  • (ii) incorporate the opinion of the Law and Judiciary Department as regards expediency of undertaking Legislation;
  • (iii) incorporate the views of the Finance Department; if any;
  • (iv) incorporate the views of other concerned Department, if obtained;
  • (v) set out all the implications of the proposed Legislation;
  • (vi) append a tentative draft Bill to Cabinet Note which shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department for drafting, if the Cabinet approves the proposal;
  • (vii) permission to make necessary changes in the draft proposal by the Law and Judiciary Department on drafting and legal point of view;
  • (viii) mention the points for the Cabinet decision. Changes suggested by Cabinet.
  • 4.4. If the Cabinet directs any changes to be made in the draft Bill or Statement of Objects and Reasons or Memorandum which is submitted for its approval, then such changes shall be carried out by the Originating Department in the draft Bill and thereafter, the proposal shall be sent to the Law and Judiciary Department. SubCommittee of Cabinet.
  • 4.5. If the Cabinet refers the draft Bill to the Sub-Committee of Cabinet, the Originating Department shall place the proposal before the Sub-Committee of Cabinet and after approval of proposal by the Sub-Committee of Cabinet/Cabinet, the Department shall forward the file to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny of draft. Forward draft Bill to Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny.
  • 4.6. (1) After Cabinet approval, the Originating Department shall prepare the draft Bill in English with Statement of Objects and Reasons, in duplicate as per the Cabinet decision and shall forward the file to the Law and Judiciary Department, for scrutiny of the draft Bill along with the Cabinet decision.
  • (2) The Originating Department shall forward two copies of the draft Bill typed on half margin with double spacing along with the file to facilitate the scrutiny thereof by the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (3) The Originating Department shall also forward soft copy of the draft Bill to the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (4) The Originating Department shall depute the officer who is conversant with the proposal for discussion while drafting and finalizing the draft Bill in the Law and Judiciary Department. Scrutiny of Bill.
  • 4.7. The Law and Judiciary Department will undertake the scrutiny of the draft Bill and will finalized English draft with Statement of Objects and Reasons and, if required, the Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation and Financial Memorandum. The draft Bill will be finalized in consultation with the officers of Originating Department. After finalization of draft, the file with draft will be submitted for approval. Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation.
  • 4.7.1. The Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation will be prepared, if the draft Bill contains the provisions of delegation of powers to the Government or other authorities of the Government. Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation will explain the scope of proposal and state whether they are of a normal or exceptional character. It will be appended to the draft Bill after Statement of Objects and Reasons.

Financial Memorandum.

  • 4.7.2. If the draft Bill contains the provisions which would involve expenditure from the Consolidated Fund of the State after its enactment, the Financial Memorandum shall be appended to the Bill. The Financial Memorandum shall be prepared by the Originating Department in consultation with the Finance Department. The Financial Memorandum will provide the estimates of the recurring and non-recurring expenditure. The draft of the Financial Memorandum will be finalized at the time of finalizing the draft Bill and will be appended to the Bill at the end. Forward draft Bill to Originating Department.
  • 4.7.3. After finalizing the English draft Bill in the Law and Judiciary Department, the file alongwith final draft Bill in English will be returned to the Originating Department for concurrence and to verify whether the draft Bill meets the requirements of the Department and is as per the Cabinet decision.

Originating Department to communicate to Law and Judiciary Department.

  • 4.7.4. If the draft Bill meets the requirements of the Originating Department, the Department shall communicate the same to the Law and Judiciary Department. If any changes/corrections are suggested by the Originating Department in the draft Bill, the Originating Department shall show the corrections in ink and the corrected copy shall be sent to the Law and Judiciary Department. Proof of Bill. 4.8. After finalization of draft, the Law and Judiciary Department will send the English draft Bill to the Government Printing Press for obtaining proof thereof. Marathi and Hindi translation of draft from Directorate of Languages.
  • 4.8.1. The Law and Judiciary Department will send two English copies of the draft Bill to the Directorate of Languages for Marathi and Hindi translation, respectively.

Proof of Bill in Marathi and Hindi.

  • 4.8.2. The Directorate of Languages will translate the English draft Bill into Marathi and Hindi and will send it to the Government Press for proof. Checking of Marathi and Hindi copies.
  • 4.8.3. The Directorate of Languages will check the Marathi and Hindi copies of the draft Bill and will send it to the Law and Judiciary Department. Proof checking of English and Marathi copies by Law and Judiciary Department.
  • 4.8.4. The Law and Judiciary Department will check the English and Marathi proofs of the draft Bill. The Marathi copy of the draft Bill will be finalized in consultation with officers of the Directorate of Languages and concerned officers of the Administrative Department. Call printed final copies from Government Press.
  • 4.8.5. After approval of English and Marathi proofs, the Law and Judiciary Department will send it to the Government Printing Press for final printing.

Final Bill copies checking.

  • 4.8.6. The Law and Judiciary Department will check the English and Marathi final copies of the draft Bill received from the Government Printing Press. Forward final Marathi and English copies to Originating Department.
  • 4.8.7. The Law and Judiciary Department will forward the English and Marathi final copies of the draft Bill to the Originating Department for checking and approval.

Concurrence from Originating Department.

  • 4.8.8. (a) The Originating Department will check the Marathi and English copies of the draft Bill, compare Marathi copy with the English copy and will communicate to the Law and Judiciary Department, for further necessary action in respect of the Bill.
  • (b) If any changes are made or suggested by the Originating Department in the draft Bill, the Department shall show the same in ink in the draft Bill. Previous sanction of President.
  • 4.9. (a) If the provisions of the Bill contain restrictions on trade, commerce and intercourse among the States, the Originating Department shall obtain the previous sanction of the President under the proviso to Article 304(b) of the Constitution.
  • (b) The necessary letter to be forwarded to the Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India, will be prepared by the Law and Judiciary Department. Governor’s Recommenda tion.
  • 4.10. (a) If the Bill contains the provisions for any of the matters specified in sub-clauses (a) to (f) of clause (1) of Article 199 of the Constitution, imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of tax, borrowing of money, guarantee by State, appropriation of moneys out of Consolidated Fund, etc; or if the Bill enacts as an Act and brought into operation, it would involve the expenditure from the Consolidated Fund of the State, the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207 of the Constitution is necessary for introduction /consideration of the Bill in the House.
  • (b) The necessary Note will be prepared by the Law and Judiciary Department to obtain the recommendation of the Governor for introduction / consideration of the Bill in the House.
  • (c) The Note will be sent to the concerned Department, for the signature of the Secretary of the Department, Minister in charge of the Bill and Chief Minster. The Note will be sent to the Secretary to the Governor, for approval of the Governor for introduction/consideration of the Bill. Submission of Notice to Minister for signature.
  • 4.11. (a) The Law and Judiciary Department will submit Notice (Annexure VI) (the motion for leave to introduce Bill in House and Marathi copy of the Bill) for the signature of the concerned Minister through the Secretary of the Originating Department.
  • (b) The Originating Department will obtain the signature of the Secretary of the Department and concerned Minister on the Notice and on the Statement of Objects and Reasons of the Marathi Bill and will return the papers to the concerned officer of the Law and Judiciary Department. Notice of Bill to State Legislature for introduction of Bill.
  • 4.12. (1) The Law and Judiciary Department will give Notice of the Bill to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council, for introduction of the Bill in the House with the following documents,-
  • (a) Motion for leave to introduce Bill in House which is signed by the concerned Minister.
  • (b) Marathi copy of the Bill which is signed by the concerned Minister.
  • (c) Letter containing the subject matter of the Bill (Entries of Lists of Seventh Schedule to the Constitution) and whether the Bill is Money Bill or not.
  • (d) Recommendation of Governor under Article 207 (1) or
  • (3) of the Constitution, if required, the copy of Order of the recommendation.
  • (e) 5 copies of Bill, Marathi, English and Hindi, each.
  • (f) Previous sanction of the President under clause (b) of article 304 of the Constitution, if any, required.
  • (2) The Notice of Bill will be given either to the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council, as the case may be.
  • (3) The copy of the Notice of the Bill will be sent to the Parliamentary Affairs Department, for information.

Intimation to Originating Department.

  • 4.13. After giving Notice of the Bill to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, the Law and Judiciary Department will inform the same to the concerned Department.

Citation of Bill.

  • 4.14. The concerned officer of the Originating Department will collect the Citation Form from the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council and will give the same to the concerned Minister for introduction and passing of the Bill. (See Citation Forms in Annexure XIX and XX). Briefing to Minister.
  • 4.15. The officers of the Originating Department will brief to the concerned Minister in respect of the provisions of the Bill and will remain present in the Gallery of the concerned House of the State Legislature while discussion on the Bill.

Introduction of Bill in Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Co uncil.

  • 4.16. The Bill will be introduced (in Marathi) in the House of the State Legislature (Assembly/Council) to whom the Notice of motion has been given.

Publication of Bill.

  • 4.17. After introduction of the Bill in the Assembly/Council, the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat will publish the Bill in the Official Gazette. The Marathi Bill will be published in Extraordinary Gazette, Part V, English Bill will be published in Part VA and Hindi in Part VII. (See Gazette parts in Annexure XVII).

Authoritative text of Bill publication by Law and Judiciary Department.

  • 4.18. After introduction of a Bill in the House, the authoritative text of the Bill in English will be published by the Law and Judiciary Department in the Official Gazette, in Part VIII, as required by Article 348(3) of the Constitution. Bill passing by both Houses.
  • 4.19. (1) If the House agrees, the Bill will be passed by the House in which it was introduced and will be transmitted to another House.
  • (2) If the other House agrees, the transmitted Bill will be passed in that House. After passing of Bill by both Houses, it is considered that the Bill is passed by both Houses. Note.-The detail procedure of Bill passing is given in Chapter VI of this Manual. Authentic copies of Bill.
  • 4.20. When the Bill is passed by both Houses of the Maharashtra State Legislature, the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat will forward the authentic copies of the Bill signed by Speaker of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly, when the Bill is introduced in the Assembly, or by Chairman of the Maharashtra Legislative Council, when the Bill is introduced in the Council, to the Law and Judiciary Department. Forwarding Authentic copies of Bill to Governor/Pre sident for assent.
  • 4.21. The Law and Judiciary Department will forward the authentic copies received from the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, to the Governor / President, as the case may be, for the assent under Article 200 of the Constitution, along with the copies of all stages of the Bill.

H 2316-6 Publication of Act.

  • 4.22. (1) When the authentic copies of the Bill are sign by the Governor / President, as the case may be, the Law and Judiciary Department will publish the Bill as an Act of the State Legislature in the Official Gazette. The English and Marathi Act will be published by the Law and Judiciary Department and Hindi Act will be published by the Directorate of Languages.
  • (2) The Marathi Act will be published in Part IV, English Act will be published in Part VIII and Hindi Act will be published in Part VII in the Official Gazette. The Hindi Act will be published by Directorate of Languages after publication of Marathi and English Act. (See Gazette parts in Annexure XVII).
  • (3) The Government Printing Press will upload all Gazette copies on egazette.mahaonline.gov.in, for information of general public. Checking Gazette copy of Act.
  • 4.23. (1) The Government Printing Press will send the Gazette copies of the Act to the Originating Department and to the Law and Judiciary Department and to the concerned offices as mentioned in the Government Resolution of the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (2) The Gazette copies shall be checked and if any printing error is noticed, the errata will be issued by the Law and Judiciary Department. Forwarding authentic copies of Bill.
  • 4.24. The authentic copies of the Bill which has been signed by the Governor or President, as the case may be, one authentic copy thereof will be retained in the Law and Judiciary Department and another copy will be returned to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat. Bringing into force provisions of Act.
  • 4.25. (1) If the Bill provides the commencement clause for bringing into force the provisions of the Act, the Originating Department will publish the notification in the Official Gazette, specifying the date from which the Act will come into force.
  • (2) The Originating Department will decide the date of commencement of the Act with the approval of the concerned Minister.
  • (3) The Originating Department will refer the notification to the Law and Judiciary Department for vetting/scrutiny. After approval of notification by the Law and Judiciary Department, the Originating Department will publish it in the Official Gazette.

Making of Rules.

  • 4.26. (1) If the Act provides to make rules, the Originating Department shall make rules within six months by following due procedure and shall publish it in the Official Gazette.
  • (2) If it is necessary to issue any Notification under the Act for implementation of the Act, the Originating Department will issue required Notification by following due procedure. Procedure of Appropriation Bill.
  • 4.27. (B) PROCEDURE OF APPROPRIATION BILL/ SUPPLEMENTARY APPROPRIATION BILL.
  • (1) The draft of Appropriation Bill will be prepared by the Finance Department. The file will be forwarded to the Law and Judiciary Department, for scrutiny of the draft Bill.
  • (2) The Law and Judiciary Department will check the Preamble of the Bill, the figures of the amounts specified in the Bill and in the Schedule of the Bill. If any corrections are found, it will show in ink and the file will be returned to the Finance Department.
  • (3) The Finance Department will send the draft Bill to Directorate of Languages for Marathi and Hindi translation.
  • (4) The English and Marathi copies of the Bill will be checked by the Finance Department. Cabinet approval.
  • (5) The Finance Department will take the approval of the Cabinet for presenting the demands before the State Legislature. M.L.A.R. 260(1)
  • (6) No demand for a grant will be made except on the recommendation of the Governor. The Parliamentary Affairs Department will submit the file to the Governor to allot a date to lay the demands for grant before the House. Bill publication.
  • (7) The Finance Department will provide printed English and Marathi copies of the Bill to the Law and Judiciary Department. The Law and Judiciary Department will get the Bill number from the office of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly. The Marathi and English Bill will be published in the Official Gazette by the Law and Judiciary Department after approval of the Finance Minister. The Bill will be published after the voting on demands by the House. The Marathi Bill will be published in Extraordinary Gazette, Part V, English Bill will be published in Part VA. H 2316-6a
  • (8) The Government Resolution will be send to the Directorate of Languages for publication of Hindi Bill in the Official Gazette. Hindi Bill will be published by the Directorate of Languages in Part VII.
  • (9) The Finance Department will obtain Governor’s recommendation for introduction of Appropriation Bill in the House. The Finance Department will communicate the Governor’s recommendation to the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (10) The Notice of Appropriation Bill will be given only to the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly. The Law and Judiciary Department will follow the same procedure of other Bills to give notice of Appropriation Bill, to the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat.
  • (11) After voting of the demands, an Appropriation Bill will be introduced in the Assembly seeking to authorize payment and appropriation of certain sums from and out of the Consolidated Fund of the State for the services and purposes during the financial year.
  • (12) After introduction of Appropriation Bill, it is not necessary to publish the Bill in Official Gazette, by the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat.
  • (13) After introduction of a Bill in the Legislative Assembly, the authoritative text of the Bill in English will be published by the Law and Judiciary Department in the Official Gazette, in Part VIII, as required by Article 348(3) of the Constitution.
  • (14) After the Bill is passed by the Legislative Assembly, it will be transmitted to the Secretary of the Legislative Council for recommendations of the Council and it will return by Council as any other Money Bill.
  • (15) The regular procedure will be followed for sending authentic copies of Bill for the assent of the Governor and publishing of an Act of the State Legislature as provided in paras 4.21 and 4.22.

Vote on Account M.L.A.R. 262.

  • 4.28. (1) Generally, a full Budget for the year including provisions for new Items is presented to the State Legislature and is passed after discussion and voting before the 31st March. On any day subsequent to the presentation of the Budget but before 31st March a motion may be made for any grant in advance in respect of the estimated expenditure for a part of any financial year.
  • (2) Such motion state the total sum required and the various amounts needed for each department or item of expenditure which composed that sum shall be stated in a Schedule appended to the motion.
  • (3) When owing to the General elections intervening or any other reason, it is not possible to present a full Budget or Appropriation Act or can’t be passed before 31st March, a Budget for standing charges and continuing schemes is presented and a ‘Vote on Account’ for the expenditure for some months is obtained, on a motion moved to that effect in the Legislative Assembly.
  • (4) The Vote on Account Bill will be passed and assented by the Governor before 31st March. The Vote on Account Act will be repealed in Appropriation Act.
  • (5) The procedure provided in Paragraph 4.27 above is applicable for processing and passing of Vote on Account Bill. Publication of Vote on Account Act/ Appropriation Act.
  • 4.29. The Vote on Account Act will be published in the Official Gazette on or before the 31st March.

Supplementary demand (Appropriation (Supplementary) Bill). M.L.A.R. 260

  • 4.30. (1) If, in the course of a financial year, it is found that the appropriations sanctioned by the Legislature under the Appropriation Act are not sufficient for the purpose of meeting the expenditure, in the Schedule to the Act, or when need arises during the course of the financial year to incur expenditure on some New Services not contemplated in the annual financial statement, it becomes necessary for the Government to approach the Legislature for its sanction to incur such expenditure. Supplementary Demands are presented to the Legislature in the course of a financial year under Article 205 of the Constitution in respect of the following types of cases :–
  • (i) for recoupment of advances sanctioned by Government from the Contingency Fund;
  • (ii) for increasing the appropriations included in the Appropriation Act, if the sanctioned appropriations are found to be insufficient for the purpose of meeting the expenditure on the particular service up to the end of the financial year;
  • (iii) for the purpose of obtaining the approval of the Legislature to an increase in the estimated cost of a substantial magnitude;
  • (iv) for New Items of expenditure involving recurring expenditure.
  • (2) The procedure provided in Paragraph 4.27 above is applicable for processing and passing of Supplementary (Appropriation) Bill.

Excess demand. Excess Expenditure Bill. M.L.A.R. 259

  • 4.31. (1) Inspite of all the care taken to make as accurate an estimation as possible of the anticipated expenditure, some unforeseen expenditure does sometimes get debited to Government account during the last few months of the financial year, which results in excesses over the sanctioned grants and appropriations. The excess expenditure which thus occurs under different heads is detected by the Accountant General after the close of the financial year and is exhibited in the Audit Report, which is submitted to the Legislature. The circumstances, under which the excess has occurred, are examined in detail by the Public Accounts Committee and if that body is satisfied, it recommends regularization of the excess expenditure. Thereafter, a statement of excess expenditure is prepared by the Government in the same manner as in the case of supplementary statement of expenditure and presented to the Legislature under Article 205(1) (b) of the Constitution. It is then dealt with in the same manner as the Supplementary demands for discussion and voting in the Assembly followed by the passing of the Appropriation Act.
  • (2) The procedure provided in Paragraph 4.27 above is applicable for processing and passing of Excess Expenditure Bill.
  • (C) PROCEDURE WHEN BILLS ARE RESERVED FOR PRESIDENTS ASSENT. Reservation of Bill for Presidents assent.
  • 4.32. If any provision of a Bill passed by the State Legislature, with respect to one of the matters enumerated in the Concurrent List (List III) of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution of India, is repugnant to the provisions of an earlier law made by Parliament or an existing law with respect to that matter, then such Bill will be required to reserve for the assent of the President under Article 254 (2) of the Constitution.
  • (1) The Law and Judiciary Department will forward the authentic copies received from the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat to the Secretary to the Governor under Article 200 of the Constitution, along with letter and ten copies of Bill of all stages, for the assent of the President.
  • (2) The copy of the assent letter will be forwarded to the Special Commissioner to the Government of Maharashtra, Maharashtra Sadan, Copernicus Road, New Delhi 110001, along with two copies of the Bill of all stages, for information and taking follow up.
  • (3) The copy of the said letter will be sent to the concerned Administrative Department of the Bill, for information.
  • (4) The Secretary to the Governor will send the authentic copies of the said Bill to the Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India, for the assent of the President. The copy of the said letter will be forwarded to,-
  • (a) the Secretary (Legislation), Law and Judiciary Department, Mantralaya,
  • (b) the Secretary to the Originating Department of the Bill, and
  • (c) the Special Commissioner to the Government of Maharashtra, Maharashtra Sadan, Copernicus Road, New Delhi 110001, for information and taking follow up. Queries/refer ence from Central Government on Bill. 4.33.

2

.

  • (1) If any query raised by any Ministry of the Government of India or references received in respect of the Bill, the letter received from the Ministry of Home Affairs (through Office of Governor) will be transferred to the Originating Department of the Bill, for taking necessary action.
  • (2) The Originating Department will prepare the justification to the queries raised by the Ministry of the Government of India and will submit the file along with letter to be forwarded to the Ministry of Home Affairs to the Law and Judiciary Department, for approval.
  • (3) After approval of letter/reply by the Law and Judiciary Department, the Secretary of the Originating Department will send the reply to the Ministry of Home Affairs. The copy of the letter will be sent to the Secretary, Law and Judiciary Department and to the Special Commissioner to the Government of Maharashtra, Maharashtra Sadan, Copernicus Road, New Delhi 110001.
  • (4) As per the instructions of the Ministry of Home Affairs, the Originating Department will send the above reply to the Ministry of Home Affairs within thirty days from the receipt of the said reference.
  • (D) WITHHOLD ASSENT TO BILL / WITHDRAWAL OF

BILL

Withhold assent to Bill.

  • 4.34. (1) If the Governor withholds the assent to the Bill under Article 200 of the Constitution or the President withholds the assent to the Bill under Article 201 of the Constitution, any communication received to the Law and Judiciary Department, will be forwarded to the concerned Originating Department of the Bill.
  • (2) The Originating Department shall inform/bring to the notice of the same to the Cabinet.
  • (3) The Law and Judiciary Department will return the authentic copies of the Bill received from Governor office/Ministry of Home Affairs, to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat and one copy will be retained in the Law and Judiciary Department. Withdrawal of Bill from the House.
  • 4.35. (a) When the Originating Department decides to withdraw a Bill from any House of State Legislature, it will take the approval of the Cabinet for such withdrawal.
  • (b) The Originating Department will prepare the motion in consultation with the Law and Judiciary Department and will send such motion to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat under the signature of the concerned Minister, for withdrawal of the Bill.

Withdrawal of authentic copy of Bill pending for assent.

  • 4.36. (a) If the Originating Department decides to withdraw a Bill, for any reason, of which authentic copy is pending before the Governor or President, as the case may be, for the assent, the Originating Department shall take the approval of the Cabinet for such withdrawal. Before submitting the proposal to Cabinet, it shall consult the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (b) After approval of the Cabinet, the Originating Department will prepare a draft letter to be forwarded for withdrawal of authentic copies of the Bill in consultation with the Law and Judiciary Department. The Department will send the letter to the Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India. Communicati on with Central Government.
  • 4.37. The communication of any reference of the Bill pending for President’s assent shall be made to the Secretary, Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India, New Delhi.

Forwarding of withdrawn authentic copies of Bill to Legislature Secretariat.

  • 4.38. The authentic copies of the Bill which has withdrawn from the Governor or President, as the case may be, will be returned to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat by the Law and Judiciary Department and one copy will be retained in the Law and Judiciary Department.

Note.–– The above procedure is generally followed in the Law and Judiciary Department for handling of Legislation work (Bills /Ordinances and Sub-ordinate Legislation work). In exceptional circumstances and urgency of work some steps are waived.

H 2316-7

ORDINANCE PROCESSING STAGES

Procedure of making reference of Ordinance to Law and Judiciary Department.

  • 5.1. When the Originating Department proposes to promulgate an Ordinance to enact a new law or to make amendments in the Act, the following procedure is followed:-

Promulgation of an Ordinance.

5.1.1

The Ordinance can be promulgated when both Houses of the State Legislature are not in Session and the Governor is satisfied that circumstances exist which render it necessary for him to take immediate action, the Governor may promulgate an Ordinance under Article 213 of the Constitution of India. Formulation of Policy/Preparat ion of proposal by Originating Department.

5.1.2

The Originating Department will before preparing any Legislative Proposal of an Ordinance, formulate the policy; it includes purpose of the Legislation, scope of Legislation, achievements by making amendments, administrative approval of the concerned Department, financial aspects, etc. Urgency to promulgate Ordinance.

5.1.3

When the Originating Department decides to promulgate an Ordinance by the Governor, the Department shall justify the need/urgency for promulgation of an Ordinance in the file. Preparation of draft Ordinance with Statement.

5.1.4

The Originating Department shall prepare the draft of the Ordinance with Statement.

Ordinance processing procedure.

5.1.5

The procedure provided in Chapter IV, in paragraph 4.1 to 4.8 (with respect to Bill), will be applicable for preparation and finalization of draft Ordinance. Forward printed copies to Department. 5.2.

After following the procedure provided in Chapter IV, in paragraph 4.1 to 4.8 (with respect to Bill), the printed copies of the draft Ordinance (English and Marathi) will be forwarded by the Law and Judiciary Department to the Originating Department, for checking and approval. Submission of draft Ordinance to Governor.

  • 5.3. If the draft Ordinance meets the requirements of the Department, the Originating Department will submit the draft Ordinance to the Governor for signature on Marathi copy (Green copy) supplied by the Law and Judiciary Department.

H 2316-7a Presidents Instructions.

  • 5.4. (1) If the provisions of the proposed Ordinance are repugnant to the provisions of any law made by Parliament or an existing law with respect to the matters enumerated in Concurrent List (List III) of the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution, it shall be necessary to obtain the Instructions of the President, for promulgation of an Ordinance.
  • (2) The Originating Department shall obtain the Instructions from the President for promulgation of an Ordinance. The necessary Letter to be forwarded to the Ministry of Home Affairs, Government of India will be finalised by the Law and Judiciary Department and will be sent to the Originating Department.
  • (3) After receiving the President’s Instructions to the Ordinance proposal, the Originating Department shall submit the draft Ordinance through the Chief Minister to Governor for his signature in consultation with the Law and Judiciary Department. Publication of an Ordinance.
  • 5.5. (1) After Governor’s signature on the Ordinance copy, the Secretary of the Originating Department shall sign below the Governor’s signature and shall immediately forward the file to the Law and Judiciary Department with necessary Note, for publication of the Ordinance in the Official Gazette.
  • (2) The Law and Judiciary Department will publish the Marathi and English Ordinance in the Official Gazette. The Hindi Ordinance will be published by the Directorate of Languages.
  • (3) The Marathi Ordinance will be published in Part IV, English in Part VIII and Hindi in part VII, in the Official Gazette. Checking of Gazette Copies of Ordinance.
  • 5.6. The Government Printing Press will send the Gazette copies of the Ordinance to the Originating Department and to the Law and Judiciary Department and to the concerned offices as mentioned in the Government Resolution of the Law and Judiciary Department. The Gazette copies will be checked and if any printing error is noticed, the errata will be issued by the Law and Judiciary Department. Postpromulgation action.
  • 5.7. As soon as, the Ordinance is published in the Official Gazette, the following action shall be taken,-
  • (a) After the publication of an Ordinance in the Official Gazette, the Law and Judiciary Department will forward the copy of the Ordinance to the Office of the Governor;
  • (b) One copy of the Ordinance to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and one copy to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislative Council, for being laid before both Houses of the Legislature as required under clause (2) of article 213 of the Constitution; and copy of the letter along with Ordinance will be send to the Parliamentary Affairs Department;
  • (c) The Originating Department shall send a letter of motion signed by concerned Minister to the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat to lay the Ordinance before both Houses of the Maharashtra State Legislature. The Law and Judiciary Department will give the format (Annexure V) of motion to lay Ordinance before both Houses of the Maharashtra State Legislature to the Originating Department. Laying of Ordinances before both Houses.
  • 5.8. The concerned Minister of the Department will lay the Ordinance before the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and the Maharashtra Legislative Council when it is in Session.

Ordinance conversion Bill.

  • 5.9. (1) The Law and Judiciary Department will take necessary steps to convert the Ordinance into Bill.
  • (2) The final English draft Bill will be prepared by the Law and Judiciary Department and will send to the Directorate of Languages for preparing Marathi and Hindi draft of the Bill.
  • (3) The procedure provided in Chapter IV in paragraphs 4.3 to
  • 4.26 relating to Bill will be applicable for preparation of draft Bill of conversion of Ordinance to publication of Act of the State Legislature in the Official Gazette. Lapse of Ordinance.
  • 5.10. The Ordinance will remain force upto six weeks from the reassembly of the State Legislature, after expiration of six weeks, it will cease to operate (See Article 213 of the Constitution). Disapproval of Ordinance by House.
  • 5.11. Before the expiration of six weeks, the Ordinance may be disapproved by House resolution passed by the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and agreed by the Maharashtra Legislative Council. Withdrawal of Ordinance.
  • 5.12. (1) The Ordinance may be withdrawn by the Governor at any time before its expiration.
  • (2) The Originating Department will take Cabinet approval for such withdrawal.
  • (3) The Draft Order will be prepared by the Law and Judiciary Department and will be supplied to the Originating Department. The Originating Department will submit the same to Governor.
  • (4) After Governor’s signature on the Order, the Secretary of the Originating Department shall sign below the Governor’s signature and shall immediately forward the file to the Law and Judiciary Department with necessary note, for publication of the Order in the Official Gazette. The Law and Judiciary Department will publish the Order in the Official Gazette (See Annexture XXII).

Repromulgation of Ordinance.

  • 5.13. (1) If the Ordinance is promulgated and the Ordinance conversion Bill is introduced in the House but could not be passed by both Houses of the State Legislature, if the Government decides to repromulgate Ordinance, it can be re-promulgated by following the same procedure as it was followed for first Ordinance.
  • (2) The Originating Department will take Cabinet approval for repromulgation of Ordinance. Contingency Fund Ordinance.
  • 5.14. (1) The Legislature of the State is not expected to be in Session throughout the year and occasions arise during the course of the year when the State Government is required to spend on the unforeseen items of expenditure. For this purpose, under Article 267 (2) of the Constitution, the State Legislature has established a “Contingency Fund” with a corpus of rupees 150 crore.
  • (2) If funds are not available to meet the expenditure of unforeseen items, such expenditure can be met by withdrawal of advance from the Contingency Fund. If sufficient amount is not available in Contingency Fund, the corpus can be increased by promulgating an Ordinance.
  • (3) All expenditure incurred by withdrawal of advance from the Contingency Fund, is required to be recouped later on by presenting Supplementary Demands or Excess Demands, as the case may be, to the Legislature.
  • (4) The Contingency Ordinance is promulgated for temporary purposes; therefore, it is not necessary to convert the Ordinance into Bill.

BILL PASSING PROCEDURE

Preliminary. 6. (1) All legislative proposals to be brought before the Maharashtra State Legislature in the form of Bills A Bill is a statute in draft and cannot become law unless it is passed by both Houses of the State Legislature and received the assent of the Governor/President thereon.

  • (2) The process of law making begins with the introduction of a Bill in either House of State Legislature. A Bill can be introduced either by a Minister or a member other than a Minister. In the former case, it is called a Government Bill and in the latter case, it is known as a Private Member's Bill (Non-Official Bill).
  • (3) The provisions of rules 111 to158 and rules 194 to 199 of the Assembly Rules and the provisions of rules 107 to153 and 189 to 194 of the Council Rules provides for the procedure of passing of Bill in the House. Notice of Bill to Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat.
  • 6.1. (1) After complying the procedure mentioned in paragraphs 4.1 to 4.11, the Law and Judiciary Department will give Notice of the Bill to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council, for introduction of a Bill in the House with the following documents,-
  • (a) Motion for leave to introduce a Bill in House which is signed by the concerned Minister;
  • (b) Marathi copy of the Bill which is signed by the concerned Minister;
  • (c) Letter containing the subject matter of the Bill (Entries of Lists of Seventh Schedule to the Constitution) and whether the Bill is Money Bill or not;
  • (d) Recommendation of Governor under Article 207 (1) or (3) of the Constitution, if required, the copy of Order of the recommendation;
  • (e) five copies of Bill, Marathi, English and Hindi, each;
  • (f) Previous sanction of the President under clause (b) of article 304 of the Constitution, if any, required.
  • (2) Subject to the provisions of Article 198 of the Constitution, the Notice of a Bill may be given either to the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly or Maharashtra Legislative Council, as the case may be, as decided by the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (3) The copy of the Notice of the Bill will be sent to the Parliamentary Affairs Department, for information. Intimation to Originating Department.
  • 6.2. After giving Notice of the Bill to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, the Law and Judiciary Department will inform the same to the concerned Department. Notice period.
  • 6.3. (1) The period of Notice of a motion, for leave to introduce a Bill under rule 111(3)/107(2) of Assembly Rules/Council Rules is fifteen days expiring on the day previous to the day on which the motion is made.
  • (2) The period of Notice may be curtailed/dispensed with by the Speaker/Chairman under rule 55/54 of Assembly Rules/Council Rules.
  • (3) To waive the period of Notice, the concerned Minister will make request to the Speaker/Chairman of the House. A letter shall be sent to the Speaker/Chairman of the House to curtail/dispense the Notice period (see Annexure VI (3)) . Priority of Bill.
  • 6.4. The Law and Judiciary Department and Parliamentary Affairs Department will communicate the priority of Bills to the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, for consideration and passing of Bills in the House. Bill Number. 6.5. The Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat will give number to the Bill. If Notice is given to the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly, the Bill number will be given by the office of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and if Notice is given to the Maharashtra Legislative Council, the Bill number will be given by the office of the Maharashtra Legislative Council. Accordingly the Bill will be called as L.A. Bill/ L.C. Bill. The copies of the Bill will be printed by the Government Printing Press on behalf of the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat. Annexure to Bill.
  • 6.6. If the Bill is to make amendments in the Act, the Annexure is appended to the Bill. The Annexure contains the existing provisions of the amended sections. The Annexure is prepared by the concerned office of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council to whom the Notice of Bill has been given. The Annexure is printed alongwith the Bill. Note.––The Annexure is useful for the Members to see the existing provisions of the Act.

Governor’s recommendat ion.

  • 6.7. If the Bill requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207 of the Constitution for introduction/consideration of Bill by the House, after receiving such recommendation from the Governor, the Notice of the Bill will be given along with the recommendation Order, to the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat. The said Order will be printed along with the Bill at the end of Bill.

Citation of Bill.

  • 6.8. The concerned officer of the Originating Department will collect the Citation Form (see Annexures XIX and XX) from the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council and will give the same to the concerned Minister for introduction and passing of Bill. Briefing to Minister.
  • 6.9. The officers of the Originating Department will brief to the concerned Minister in respect of the provisions of the Bill and will remain present in the Gallery of the concerned House while discussion on the Bill. Introduction of Bill.
  • 6.10. (1) The Bill will be introduced (in Marathi) in the House of the State Legislature (Assembly/Council) to whom the Notice of Bill has been given. Note.–– A Bill or amendment making provision for any of the matters specified in sub-clauses (a) to (f) of clause (1) of Article 199 shall be introduced in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly and shall not be introduced in the Maharashtra Legislative Council.
  • (2) The Bill will be shown in the Order of Day of the House.
  • (3) If the Bill requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207(1) of the Constitution, the Bill can’t be introduced in the House unless Governor’s recommendation is received.
  • (4) If the Bill requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207(3) of the Constitution, a Bill can be introduced in any House. However, it can’t be considered by the House until the Governor’s recommendation is received.
  • (5) The Maharashtra Appropriation Bill, the Maharashtra (Supplementary) Appropriation Bill, the Maharashtra Appropriation (Vote on Account) Bill, the Maharashtra Appropriation (Excess Expenditure) Bill, leving or reducing tax rates, are the money Bills, therefore, introduced in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly only.

H 2316-8

  • (6) If leave to introduce a Bill is granted by the House, the concerned Minister of the Bill will read the Motion for introduction of the Bill and the Bill will be introduced in the House. Publication of Bill by MLS.
  • 6.11. After introduction of the Bill in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly/Council, the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat will publish the Bill in the Official Gazette. The Marathi Bill will be published in Extraordinary Gazette, Part V, English Bill will be published in Part VA and Hindi in Part VII.

Note.––The Maharashtra Appropriation Bill, the Maharashtra (Supplementary) Appropriation Bill, the Maharashtra Appropriation (Vote on Account) Bill, the Maharashtra Appropriation (Excess Expenditure) Bill, are published in the Official Gazette by the Law and Judiciary Department as per the provisions of rules 116(1) and 117 of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly, before its introduction in the House, therefore these Bills are not published by the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, after its introduction in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly.

Authoritative text of Bill publication by Legislation Branch.

  • 6.12. After introduction of the Bill in House, the authoritative text in English of the Bill will be published in the Official Gazette, by Legislation Branch of the Law and Judiciary Department under Article 348(3) of the Constitution, in Part VIII. Passing of Bill by Assembly.
  • 6.13. (1) After introduction of a Bill in the Legislative Assembly (called the Assembly Bill), the “First stage” consists of discussion on the Bill and generally on any one of the following motion,-
  • (a) that the Bill be taken into consideration; or
  • (b) that Bill be referred to a Select Committee composed of such number of members of the Assembly and with instructions to report before such date, as may be specified in the motion; or
  • (c) that the Bill be referred to a Joint Committee of both Houses, composed of such number of members and with instructions to report before such date, as may be specified in the motion ; or
