Atre, Legislative Drafting-Shaping the Law for the New Millennium-T.K.Viswanathan, Principles of Legislation and Legislative draftingN.K. Chakrabarti.

GENERAL GUIDELINES FOR DRAFTSMAN

Knowledge to Draftsman.

  • 12.1 (A) Knowledge to Draftsman:- The quality of a legislation depends upon the knowledge and competence of draftsman, therefore draftsman should,––
  • (1) have full and intricate knowledge of Indian Laws and of Constitution of India, particularly fundamental rights, distribution of legislative powers between Central and State, constitutional procedures, etc.;
  • (2) be familiar with the legal principles developed or expounded by courts;
  • (3) familiar with the rules of construction adopted by courts;
  • (4) have knowledge about general principles of interpretation;
  • (5) have knowledge about English grammar;
  • (6) have knowledge of legislative procedure;
  • (7) have knowledge of Administration, Court working and life of community;
  • (8) familiar with the General Clauses Act, (Central and State Acts);
  • (9) have knowledge of allocation of executive function of State machinery, public authorities, their powers and functions;
  • (10) have knowledge about administrative machinery/ system;
  • (11) familiar with legislative precedents;
  • (12) know the policy of subject of legislation and also the practical application and social impact of the subject;
  • (13) know the audience (to whom law is to be made applicable);
  • (14) prepare plan how to draft the legislation. Qualities with draftsman.
  • 12.2 (B) Qualities with draftsman:-The draftsman should have the following qualities :––
  • (1) Possess a systematic mind, orderliness in the formulation of thoughts, ability to pay meticulous attention to detail and to work accurately under pressure.

H 2316-15a

  • (2) Familiar with language and methods, the technique employed in body of legislation of which his own work is destined to form of part.
  • (3) Have critical and imaginative mind.
  • (4) Able to work with colleagues and those skilled in other discipline and to give and take constructive criticism and advice.
  • (5) Be endowed with common sense of social awareness.
  • (6) Have sense of use of language, precise conception of the objects desired and knowledge of technical interpretation which are placed by law on certain forms of languages.
  • (7) Have aim of clarity, conciseness and comprehensive coverage rather than aesthetic beauty.
  • (8) Able to reduce doubts, ambiguities and foreseeable difficulties.
  • (9) Have ability to understand, think, classify, arrange, patch or build above all on paper scheme.
  • (10) Must have good basic knowledge, technique to use proper English word, command on English language.
  • (11) Must have critical ability, lots of imagination and plenty of practice of drafting legislations.
  • (12) Must familiar with language and methods the technique employed in body of legislation of which his own work is destined to form of part.
  • (13) Must have constructive imagination, the power to visualize things in concrete and to foresee whether and how a scheme of the Act will work out in practice.
  • (14) Should well conversant with law.
  • (15) Should possess mastery of the requisite technique of drafting of legislations.
  • (16) Must have knowledge of judicial pronouncements, judicial precedents and legal principles as well as important statutory provisions for they have to understand and weigh the implications of every word used in a Bill.
  • (17) He should have enough time to digest their own drafts and consider the implications of what they have drafted.
  • (18) Know that, perfect Bill has never been drafted and never will be, his draft is final because nobody is over him to check the draft.
  • (19) Able to handle time pressure to complete the work.
  • (20) Have ability to accept the criticism and to improve in future.
  • (21) Able to reduce doubt, ambiguity and unreasonable problems which would arise, his success will depend upon the extent to which he achieves the end.
  • (C) General guidelines for draftsman. General guidelines for draftsman.
  • 12.3 (1) Words are the skin of the language. Language opens up the way of the makers mind. The draftsman should employ appropriate phraseology to attain the object of legislative policy which it seeks to achieve.
  • (2) A draftsman must have a clear idea of what he is required to draft. If his ideas are confused his language cannot be clear. In order to clarify his ideas and sometimes of the policy makers, a draftsman should discuss the legislative proposal with officers of the Administrative Department/Ministry and clear up any points which are obscure.
  • (3) The arts of draftsman consists of sense of use of language, precise conception of objects, and desired knowledge of technical interpretation. Therefore, the draft should be clear, precise and in simple language.
  • (4) The Draftsman shall strictly follow the precedents. The precedents can be discovered in the existing Acts where similar provisions have been made.
  • (5) The draftsman should see that the provision if required to be interpreted, do not result in the inadvertent consequence of someone getting benefit from his own wrong.
  • (6) In an attempt to give in depth meaning to the provisions, the draftsman very often uses language which makes the legislation incomplete. Draftsman should be careful in such matters. When two interpretations are possible one that subserves the object of the Statute will be accepted by the Court. Therefore, the draftsman should choose appropriate phrase.
  • (7) The draftsman must remember that where he provides that a particular thing should be done in a particular manner then, it has to be done in that manner and in no other manner. Very often this requirement is lost sight of by the draftsman leading to confusion and misinterpretation.
  • (8) The draftsman should see that provisions/sections of the Act are separate and unless essential are not inconsistent with each other. They should be easily separable so that if one of it is read down by the Court other provisions can stand separately on their own. Where the provision cannot be separated and delinked then the whole set of provisions will be read down as being ultra vires the object of the Act.
  • (9) The draftsman should always take care that the provisions of the Act are not drafted in isolation of one another. If the provisions are drafted in isolation then, the Courts are bound to interpret the provisions in isolation and the result will be reading down of the provisions or they being interpreted differently totally against or opposite the legislative intent.
  • (10) The draftsman should be careful while drafting express provision in the Act. Because the express provision of the Act will be interpreted in the light of express intention and will not be given restrictive or enlarge meaning.
  • (11) The draftsman should always be aware that grammatical construction leads to absurdity or injustice should be avoided because no literary meaning can be construed in such circumstances.
  • (12) The draftsman should be particular about to avoid using construction capable of causing mischievous consequences. Because it is a settled proposition that if the language of the legislation is capable of more than one interpretation, the one which is capable of causing mischievous consequences should be averted.
  • (13) The draftsman should see that the legislative intent or object of the legislation is spelt clearly and unambiguously leaving no doubt in the minds of the reader, the lawyer as well as the judiciary.
  • (14) The draftsman should be careful in drafting amendments or subsequent provisions in relation to the existing Act, because subsequent provisions based on erroneous assumption about pre-existing law which will be disregarded by the courts while ascertaining legislative intent.
  • (15) A draftsman should provide for all contingencies which are likely to arise. He should have what is called “divine prescience”. Failure on the part of a draftsman to foresee certain contingencies has sometimes led the courts to adopt strange interpretations.
  • (16) The draftsman shall prepare first draft and shall revise it until he is satisfied.
  • (17) Generally, several drafts are prepared. The draftsman shall preserve all drafts. They indicate the working of the mind of the draftsman and are useful when any question arises why a particular provision has been put in a particular form in the final draft.
  • (18) The Draftsman shall show the draft to his colleague or third person, who know the drafting and may accept the required suggestions. The more meticulous scrutiny of draft and more exacting the criticism, the better makes the legislation. Equipment’s Tools of Draftsman.
  • 12.4 (D) Equipment’s/Tools of Draftsman.-The draftsman should have knowledge of ,––

(1) Constitution of India. (2) Maharashtra General Clauses Act. (3) General Clauses Act (Central Act). (4) Judgments of High Courts and Supreme Court. (5) Jurisprudence. (6) Administrative Law. (7) Interpretation of Statutes. (8) Law Dictionary. (9) Legal Maxims.

  • (10) Information of Central Acts.
  • (11) Information of State Acts.
  • (12) Drafting experience, experience of legislature work. _______________________________________________________________________________ Note:- Some of the contents of this Chapter are taken from the Books of Legislative DraftingB.E. Atre, Legislative Drafting-Shaping the Law for the New Millennium-T.K.Viswanathan, Principles of Legislation and Legislative draftingN.K.Chakrabarti.

CLASSIFICATION OF BILLS /ACTS

  • (A) Classification of Bills Classification of Bills.

Bills may be classified as Government Bills and Private Members’ Bills.

  • 13.1 On the basis of the contents, the Bills may be classified into:-
  • (a) Original Bill.- The Bill embodying new proposals, ideas, or new policies is called Origional Bill.
  • (b) Amending Bill.- The Bill which seeks to modify, amend or revise existing Acts is called Amending Bill.
  • (c) Consolidating Bills.- The Bill which seeks to consolidate the existing laws on a particular subject is called Consolidating Bill.
  • (d) Expiring laws Bill.- The Bill to continue an expiring Act called the Expiring Laws Bill.
  • (e) Repealing Bill.-The Bill to repeal of existing Acts is called Repealling Bill.
  • (f) Ordinance conversion Bill.-The Bill which convert the Ordinace into Act (the Bill contains the same provisions of Ordinance, except technical changes are made) is called Ordinance conversion Bill.
  • (g) Money Bill.-The Bill which contains matters specified in article 199 of the Constitution, for imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax, appropriation of moneys out of the Consolidated Fund of the State, etc. is called Money Bill.
  • (h) Financial Bill.-Financial Bill is divided in two categories, in first category, the Bill provides for any matters specified for measure of Money Bill but do not consist solely of those matters, other matters are also included in that Bill; and in second category, if law enacted and brought into force, it would involve expenditure from Consolidated Fund of State. Money Bill. 13.2 Money Bill.- (1) As per provisions of Article 199(1) of the Constitution of India, a Bill containing the following matters is the Money Bill,––

H 2316-16

  • (a) the imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax;
  • (b) the regulation of the borrowing of money or the giving of any guarantee by the State, or the amendment of the law with respect to any financial obligations undertaken or to be undertaken by the State;
  • (c) the custody of the Consolidated Fund or the Contingency Fund of the State, the payment of moneys into or the withdrawal of moneys from any such Fund;
  • (d) the appropriation of moneys out of the Consolidated Fund of the State;
  • (e) the declaring of any expenditure to be expenditure charged on the Consolidated Fund of the State, or the increasing of the amount of any such expenditure;
  • (f) the receipt of money on account of the Consolidated Fund of the State or the public account of the State or the custody or issue of such money; or
  • (g) any matter incidental to any of the matters specified in sub-clauses (a) to (f).
  • (2) Money Bill can be introduced only in the Legislative Assembly and requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207(1) of the Constitution, for its introduction. Financial Bills. 13.3 Financial Bills.-The Financial Bills are divided into two categories,-
  • (a) Financial Bill of Category ‘A’.-The Bill which make provision for any of the matters specified in Money Bill but do not consist solely of those matters. This Bill shall be introduced only in the Legislative Assembly and requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207(1) of the Constitution, for its introduction.
  • (b) Financial Bill of Category ‘B’.–The Bill if enacted and brought into operation, would involve expenditure from the Consolidated Fund of the State. This Bill can be introduced in either House and it does not require recommendation of Governor for its introduction. However, a recommendation of the Governor under Article 207(3) of the Constitution is require for its consideration.

Publication of Bill.

  • 13.4 The legislation is introduced before the State Legislature/ Parliament in the form of Bill, after it is passed by the State Legislature/Parliament and assented by the Governor/President, it is published as an Act of the State Legislature /Parliament in the Official Gazette.

Salient features of Bill.

  • 13.5 Generally the following are the salient features of the Bill.
  • (1) Long title.
  • (2) Preamble.
  • (3) Enacting formula.
  • (4) Short title.
  • (5) Extent clause.
  • (6) Commencement clause.
  • (7) Duration clause.
  • (8) Definition clause.
  • (9) Declaratory clause.
  • (10) Rule making clause.
  • (11) Repeal and saving clause.
  • (12) Schedules.
  • (13) Statement of Objects and Reasons.
  • (14) Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation.
  • (15) Financial Memorandum.
  • (16) Annexures. Note 1.- The details of certain clauses are explained below and others clauses are explained in Chapter VIII. Note 2.- Clause.- In Bill, the “section” is called as “clause” and “sub-section” is called as “sub-clause”. Note 3.- When the Bill is published as an Act of the State Legislature, the “clause” of the Bill is called as “section” and “sub-clause” is called as “sub-section. ”

H 2316-16a The following are required to be printed along with the Bill. Statement of Objects and Reasons.

  • 13.6 (1) Statement of Objects and Reasons.-The Statement of Objects and Reasons is appended to each Bill introduced in the House. The Statement of Objects and Reasons briefly explains the purpose of the proposed legislation in short. The Statement is explanatory of the contents and objectives of a Bill and helps in understanding the necessity and scope of the Bill. It gives an idea or background of the provisions of the Bill or Amendments. It is, therefore, necessary that the Statement of Objects and Reasons to be drafted in a non-technical language.

Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation.

  • 13.7 (2) Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation.-The Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation is appended to the Bill, providing the details of the powers delegated by the Legislature to the Government or to the Government Authorities. The Memorandum Regarding Delegated Legislation draws attention to the scope of the proposal and also states whether delegation is of a normal or exceptional character.

Financial Memorandum.

  • 13.8 (3) Financial Memorandum.-The Financial Memorandum is appended to the Bill, where financial implications are involved. It provides the recurring or non-recurring expenditure which would involve after enactment of law.

Annexure. 13.9 (4) Annexure.- When certain sections of the parent Act are sought to be amended, the text thereof is generally appended to every Bill in the form of Annexure. The Annexure facilitates better understanding of provision of amendments. It shows effect of the amendments embodied in the Bill.

(B) Classification of Acts

Classification of Acts.

  • 13.10 (1) The Acts are enacted by the Parliament and State Legislatures on the subject matter allotted in the Lists provided in the Seventh Schedule to the Constitution of India.
  • (2) The Acts are enacted by the State Legislature on the subjects provided in Lists II and III and under articles where power to enact laws is conferred on the State Legislature.

Principal Act. 13.11 The Principal Act embodying new proposals, ideas, or new policies of the Government. The Principal Act contains all the provisions of the subjects for which purpose the Act is enacted. The Principal Act is an Act that contains substantive provisions that regulate or prohibit conduct or other activities. It may in some cases also contain amendments or repeals of other legislations. Amending Act.

  • 13.12 When Amendments are made in the existing Act is called the Amending Act. The Amending Act contains the amendments made in one Act or more than one Acts. It includes addition, substitution, insertion or deletion of provisions by way of amendments in the Principal Act, by introducing a Bill before the State Legislature or Parliament. Perpetual Act. 13.13 The Perpetual Act is a Principal Act. The Act is perpetual when no time is provided for its duration and such Act remains in force until its repeal by express or implied. A perpetual Act is not perpetual in the sense that it cannot be repealed; it is perpetual in the sense that it is not abrogated by efflux of time. Temporary Act.
  • 13.14 When the Act is enacted for temporary period or its duration is only for a specified time, is called a temporary Act. Such Act applies only for the period for which it is enacted. After the expiry of the duration or efflux of time, it becomes inoperative or it exhaust. Repealing Act. 13.15 The purpose of repealing of Act is to remove the outdated, obsolete, exhausted or temporary Acts from the statutes book. The Acts are repealed by enacting new law by the Legislature. After repeal of Act, the Act ceases to exist. Re-enactment Act.
  • 13.16 The Act which is repealed is again re-enacted with or without modification is called Re-enacted Act. Sometimes when the Act is struck down by the Court, the Legislature enacts a new law on the same subject by removing the defect of the earlier law, comes in this category. Validating Act/Legislation.
  • 13.17 Validating legislation removes the causes of ineffectiveness or invalidity of action or proceedings. The Legislature has power to enact laws having retrospective operation. The object of validating legislation is to enact a valid piece of legislation that removes the defect or vice in the earlier legislation.

The purpose of validating legislation is to declare valid, what has been held invalid by courts and once the Legislature declare such action valid, all steps taken in connection therewith are validated to the extent of validation. The actions of the Government or Government authorities are validated in this category of Act.

NON-OFFICIAL BILLS

The Bill which is other than the Government Bill, introduced by any Member of the House in the Maharashtra State Legislature is called the Non-official Bill.

Procedure of Non-Official Bill

Non-Official Bill. R.111/107, MLA/MLC Rules. 14 Any Member of the House (other than a Minister or Advocate General) may give Notice of his intention to introduce Bill in the House. A Member of the House gives Notice alongwith copy of Bill with Statement of Objects and Reasons. Copy of Bill to P.A.D.

  • 14.1 The Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat forwards the copy of the Bill to the Parliamentary Affairs Department for examining the legal and constitutional feasibility of the Bill.
  • 14.2 The Parliamentary Affairs Department shall send the copy of the Non-official Bill received from the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, to the Law and Judiciary Department for examining the legal and constitutional feasibility of the Bill and also shall forward the copy of the Bill to the concerned Administrative Department to which the Bill pertains, for information. Opinion of L.& J.D.
  • 14.3 The Law and Judiciary Department will examine the legal and constitutional feasibility of the Non-official Bill and shall forward the opinion to the Parliamentary Affairs Department.
  • 14.4 Parliamentary Affairs Department shall forward the opinion of the Law and Judiciary Department to the Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat and copy to the concerned Administrative Department, for information.
  • 14.5 A detailed note giving the relevant information in respect of Bill, the subject matter of Bill should be put up by the Administrative Department to the Secretary and the Ministerin-Charge.
  • 14.6 The Administrative Department officials shall send the relevant papers to the Private Secretary to the Minister-inCharge for submitting them to the Minister at the appropriate time.
  • 14.7 The concerned Administrative Department shall prepare the Cabinet Note and place the proposal of Bill for Cabinet approval when the Bill is selected in the Ballet for consideration.
  • 14.8 The final decision on Bill is taken by the Council of Ministers.
  • 14.9 If the Bill requires the recommendation of the Governor under Article 207 of the Constitution, the Parliamentary Affairs Department shall obtain the recommendation of the Governor and shall communicate to the Maharashtra State Legislature.
  • 14.10 The Bill will be introduced by the Member who has given the Notice of Bill to the House.
  • 14.11 If the Bill is selected in the Ballet, the Bill will be shown for consideration in the House on the Non-Official business day in the session.
  • 14.12 The discussion is held on the Bill in the House. Generally the Non-Official Bills are not passed by the House.

SUBORDINATE LEGISLATION (RULES, NOTIFICATION, ETC.)

  • (I) Subordinate Legislations Subordinate Legislation.
  • 15. The Constitution of India, as well as, laws made by Parliament and State Legislature usually vests the powers to Government to make rules, regulations, bye-laws, notifications, orders, etc., and notify it in the Official Gazette, to subserve the objectives of the main legislation. The rules, regulations, bye-laws, notifications, etc., are statutory in character, they come within the scope of what is termed as “subordinate legislation”. Normally, administrative or procedural matters are delegated to the executive authorities, Government or municipal or local authorities to whom the rule making power is confirmed by the Legislature. The procedure for making subordinate legislation will depend upon the provisions, if any, of the enabling Act under which it is made. Forms of subordinate legislation.

Forms of subordinate legislation.- The subordinate legislations are generally made in the following forms:-

Rules. 15.1 Rules.- The expression “rule” is defined in clause (39) of section 3 of the Maharashtra General Clauses Act, as a “"rule" shall mean a rule made in exercise of a power conferred by any enactment, and shall include a regulation made as a rule under any enactment”. Rules are made under the Act to make the Act more effective. Rules provide the details which have not been provided in the Act. Rules deal with the procedural matters providing for the administrative machinery. When the rules are published in the Official Gazette, it is published in the form of notification. Regulations. 15.2 Regulations.- Regulations are somewhat inferior to rules because they are generally made by authority subordinate to Government, like a Board or other statutory body or authority functioning under a statute. Regulations are made generally by authorities subordinate to the Administrative Department. Sometimes the power to make rules as well as regulations is vested in the same authority.

H 2316-17 Regulations provide set of procedure in respect of several matters. In such a case, the apparent distinction between the two powers is that while the rules deal with major problems arising under the Act, the regulations are to provide for minor and subsidiary matters. Regulations made in exercise of a statutory power, amounts to a “rule” within the meaning of section 24 of the General Clauses Act, 1897.

Notification. 15.3 Notification.- Notification is a statutory instrument issued in exercise of the powers conferred under the Act. Generally it is published in the Official Gazette, so that the public may know it. The notification is issued for appointment of authorities, constitution of committees, appointment of members on the authorities and committees constituted under the Act. Notifications are issued by the authority to which the power to issue notification is conferred by the Act. Order. 15.4 Order.- An Order, broadly speaking, is specific and may be limited in its application. The form of an order is used for notifying an action taken by the Government in accordance with the directions or powers conferred by an Act. The Order may be administrative or legislative in nature. Whether the order of the Government falls within the legislative or administrative depends on the type and quantum of administrative discretion vested in the administrative authority and also on its contents. Generally appointments are made by order; certain declarations are made by order. All orders are not always published in the Official Gazette, unless the Act specifically provides for. Bye-laws. 15.5 Bye-laws.- Bye-laws are also types of subordinate legislation and termed so, when they are framed by the Municipal bodies, Corporations or other local self-governments. Even though they are not different from rules or regulations, they are termed so to easily identify them from the rules and regulations when they are framed by the local self-governments under the powers conferred on them by an Act. Generally local authorities, corporations, local-self government bodies, etc., provide their procedural matters, under the power conferred on them by the Act or under the rules.

Scheme. 15.6 Scheme.- Scheme is a form of Subordinate Legislation containing a body of rules binding on person with whom the rules are concerned and in such a case, if made by an authority having the necessary power to do so, and enforceable in courts of law.

When any scheme is to be provided under any Act, the power is delegated to the Government or authorities to frame the scheme for specific purpose.

(II) Procedure of making referce of Subordinate Legislation

to Law and Judiciary Department. Statutory Notification, Rules, Orders, etc., refer to Law and Judiciary Department.

  • 15.7 Whenever it is proposed in any Administative Department:-
  • (a) to issue statutory rules, notifications or orders by Administative Departments under Act; or
  • (b) to submit to the Central Government any draft statutory rules, notification or order for issue by them under Central Acts,
  • (c) amendments to any rules, notifications or orders,

the draft of the same shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department for opinion or vetting or revision.

Circulars, Administrat ive Orders/Lett ers to be issued by Originating Department.

  • 15.8 The Circulars, Administrative Orders/Letters to be issued by Administrative Department shall not be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny, unless specific legal point is raised for the advice of the Law and Judiciary Department. The wordings of such Circulars, Administrative Orders/Letters shall be finalized by the Department itself.

The Law and Judiciary Department does not make scrutiny of such Circulars, Administrative Orders/Letters to be issued by Administrative Department.

Regulations and Byelaws to be issued by Subordinate Authorities.

  • 15.9 The Regulations and By-laws to be issued by the Subordinate Authorities of the Administrative Department of Mantralaya, shall not be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny, unless specific legal point is raised for the consideration/scrutiny of the Law and Judiciary Department. The wordings of such Regulations and Bye-laws shall be finalized by the Authorities or Department itself.

H 2316-17a Procedure of making reference relating to scrutiny of statutory Notification, Rules, Orders, etc.

  • 15.10 (1) The Administrative Department shall ensure before forwarding any statutory Notification, Rules, Orders, etc., to the Law and Judiciary Department for vetting or scrutiny,-
  • (a) obtain the approval of Secretary of the Department and, if deemed by the Secretary of the Department, from the concerned Minister-in-charge;
  • (b) if the proposal affects the policy of other Department or on administrative grounds, it requires the approval of other Departments; the Originating Department shall consult all other concerned Administrative Departments;
  • (c) follow the model of existing rules, notifications or orders on the same or similar subjects to prepare the draft rules, notifications or orders, as the case may be;
  • (d) forward two copies of draft in English typed on half margin with double spacing along with the file for vetting/scrutiny;
  • (e) forward up-to-date copy of the rules, notifications or orders proposed to be amended along with the file;
  • (f) state the point or points, if any, in connection of the draft which requires special examination by the Law and Judiciary Department.
  • (2) If the file is not complied as above, the file may be returned to the Department for necessary compliance. Requirement of consultation/ approval.
  • 15.11 (1) When previous consultation/approval /sanction/ permission of the authorities/ bodies is required, by the Act before making any rules, the concerned Department shall consult/ take approval/ sanction/permission of the concerned authorities/ bodies, before making such rules.
  • (2) Such files shall not be referred to the Law and Juduciary Department until the said requirement is complied by the Department.

Making of rules under Act.

  • 15.12 (1) If the Act provides the power of the Government to make rules, the Administrative Department shall prepare the draft rules as required by the Act and shall refer the file alongwith draft to the Law and Judiciary Department, for scrutiny/vetting.
  • (2) The Law and Judiciary Department will make scrutiny of the rules in consultation with the Administrative Department officers.
  • (3) As soon as, the rules are received from the Law and Judiciary Department, the Administrative Department shall, after approval of the Government, publish it in the Official Gazette.
  • (4) Administrative Department shall send the Gazette copies of the rules to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, to lay before both Houses of the State Legislature/Committee on Subordinate Legislation.
  • (5) The Administrative Department shall follow the procedure for laying down of rules/notifications before both Houses of the State Legislature as provided in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly Rules/ Maharashtra Legislative Council Rules and Circulars issued by the Parliamentary Affairs Department. Prepublication of rules.
  • 15.13 Where an Act provides the rules shall be made after previous publication, the draft rules shall be published in the Official Gazette, for information of persons likely to be affected thereby. The provisions of section 24 of the Maharashtra General Clauses Act (I of 1904) shall apply for making such rules.
  • 15.14 (1) The Originating Department shall,-
  • (a) prepare draft rules and shall refer it to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny/vetting;
  • (b) as soon as, the rules received from the Law and Judiciary Department, it shall be published in Official Gazette, for inviting objections and suggestions within specified period. As per recommendations of Committee on Subordinate Legislation of Parliament, a period of not less than thirty clear days should invariably be given to the public to send their comments/suggestions on draft rules;
  • (c) Forward Gazette copies of rules to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, to lay before the both Houses of the State Legislature/Committee on Subordinate Legislation;
  • (d) on expiry of the specified period, consider the objections and suggestions, if any, received (including Committee on Subordinate Legislation);
  • (e) finalize the rules and shall refer it again to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny;
  • (f) after approval from the Law and Judiciary Department publish final rules in the Official Gazette;
  • (g) send final Gazette copies of the rules to the Secretary, Maharashtra Legislature Secretariat, to lay before both Houses of the State Legislature/the Committee on Subordinate Legislation.
  • (2) The above procedure shall be followed while making amendments in rules.

Time frame for framing rules.

  • 15.15 (1) If the Act specifically provides to make rules, regulations and bye-laws, within specified period, the Administrative Department shall prepare rules, regulations and bye-laws, within that period. If the time limit is not provided in the Act, it shall be made within a period of six months from the date of relevant Act comes into force. In cases where, for any reason, this is not possible; it shall be brought to the notice of the concerned Secretary and the Minister at the earliest.
  • (2) After rules, regulations and bye-laws are finalized in consultation with the Law and Judiciary Department, the concerned Department shall take steps to publish it in the Official Gazette.

(III) Procedure of making recruitment Rules. Procedure of making recruitment Rules.

  • 15.16 Recruitment Rules and conditions of service of persons appointed to public services and posts in connection with the affairs of the State are made by the Act of the State Legislature; or if the Act is not made then, such rules shall be made under the proviso to article 309 of the Constitution.

The Governor or the person authorised by the Governor is empowered to make such rules for recruitment.

Recruitment Rules under article 309 of Constitution.

  • 15.17 (1) If the recruitment rules to be made under the proviso to article 309 of the Constitution of India, the Administrative Department shall prepare the draft recruitment rules which shall be approved, by the Secretary of the Department and, if deemed appropriate by the Secretary of the Department, from the concerned Minister-in-charge.
  • (2) The Administrative Department while preparing new recruitment rules or making amendments in the existing recruitment rules, shall refer the file to the General Administration Department first for approval/advice.
  • (3) The Administrative Department shall, in case of posts which are within the preview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, while preparing new recruitment rules or amendments in the existing recruitment rules, the draft rules which has approved by the General Administration Department, shall be referred to the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, for its concurrence/advice: Provided that the posts which are exempted from the purview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission (Exemption from Consultation) Regulation, 1965, the recruitment rules of such posts shall not be referred to the Maharashtra Public Service Commission.
  • (4) The draft recruitment rules approved by the General Administration Department and by the Maharashtra Public Service Commission shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny/vetting.
  • (5) The draft recruitment rules approved by the Law and Judiciary Department shall be submitted to the Governor, for approval.
  • (6) After Governor’s approval, Administrative Department shall publish the recruitment rules in the Official Gazette.
  • (7) The Administrative Department shall follow the Circulars, Orders, etc., issued by the General Administration Department, from time to time, with respect to recruitment rules and conditions of service. Recruitment Rules under Act.
  • 15.18 (1) If the Act provides the recruitment rules and conditions of service of persons appointed to public services and posts in connection with the affairs of the State shall be made under the provisions of the Act, the Administrative Department shall prepare the draft recruitment rules which shall be approved by the Secretary of the Department and, if deemed appropriate by the Secretary of the Department, from the concerned Ministerin-charge.
  • (2) The Administrative Department shall, while preparing new recruitment rules, refer the file to the General Administration Department first for approval/advice.
  • (3) The Administrative Department shall, where the posts which are within the preview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, while preparing new recruitment rules or amendments in the recruitment rules/ conditions of service, the draft rules which has been approved by the General Administration Department, refer to the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, for its concurrence/advice: Provided that, the posts which are exempted from the purview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission (Exemption from Consultation) Regulation, 1965, the recruitment rules of such posts shall not be referred to the Maharashtra Public Service Commission.
  • (4) The draft recruitment rules approved by the General Administration Department and by the Maharashtra Public Service Commission shall be referred to the Law and Judiciary Department, for scrutiny/vetting.
  • (5) The draft recruitment rules approved by the Law and Judiciary Department shall be submitted to the concerned Minister of the Department, for approval.
  • (6) After approval from the concerned Minister, the Administrative Department shall publish the recruitment rules in the Official Gazette as provided in the Act.
  • (7) The Administrative Department shall follow the Circulars, Orders, etc., issued by the General Administration Department, from time to time, with respect to recruitment rules and conditions of service. Laying of rules, notification, etc., before Committee on Subordinate Legislation. M.L.A.R 220
  • 15.19 (1) The rules, regulations, bye-laws, notifications, etc., made by the Administrative Department or any authority subordinate thereto, in exercise of the powers conferred by the Constitution, or under any Central Act or under Act of the State Legislature; and which are required to be laid before the House of the State Legislature, the Administrative Department shall lay such rules, regulations, bye-laws, notifications, etc., before the State Legislature, as soon as possible, after they are made.
  • (2) The Parliamentary Affairs Department has issued Orders and Circulars from, time to time, providing procedure for laying down of rules, regulations, bye-laws, notifications, etc., before Houses of the State Legislature and Committee on Subordinate Legislation. Each Administrative Department shall follow the same.

CHECK LIST FOR SUBORDINATE LEGISLATION

Checklist for Recruitment Rules:-

Recruitment Rules under Article 309 of Constitution.

  • 16.1 (A) Recruitment Rules under proviso to Article 309 of Constitution of India.-The following aspects shall be checked while scrutinizing the draft recruitment rules to be made under the proviso to Article 309 of the Constitution of India,-
  • (i) whether the recruitment rules are approved by the General Administration Department;
  • (ii) whether the recruitment rules are approved by the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, unless the posts are exempted from the purview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission under the Maharashtra Public Service Commission (Exemption from Consultation) Regulation, 1965;
  • (iii) if the proposal is for amending the existing recruitment rules, whether the Gazette copy (including amendments made thereto) or updated copy of existing recruitment rules, is forwarded alongwith file;
  • (iv) the mode of recruitment (by nomination/ promotion/ deputation, transfer, etc); and the ratio for the same and provision of relaxation of rules, if necessary;
  • (v) whether the recruitment rules are for whole cadre or for certain posts of the cadre; if the rules are for certain posts of the cadre, check the recruitment rules of lower posts and higher posts of the said cadre;
  • (vi) the hierarchy of all posts in the cadre;
  • (vii) whether the posts are Gazetted or Non-Gazetted;
  • (viii) Group of posts (Group A, Group B, Group C or Group D posts);
  • (ix) in case the appointment is by nomination, then,-
  • (a) to check the minimum and maximum age limit, educational qualifications and experience for the post and relaxation of age provision;

H 2316-18

  • (b) probation period for the post;
  • (x) the definition clause, whether terms used in rules are defined;
  • (xi) Computer Operations Certificate rule;
  • (xii) Marathi and Hindi Examinations rule;
  • (xiii) Small Family Declaration rule;
  • (xiv) Departmental Examination rule, if required;
  • (xv) provision for transfer of the candidates as per the requirement of rules;
  • (xvi) whether the provisions are arbitrary/violative of article 14 of the Constitution. Recruitment rules under Central or State Act.
  • 16.2. (B) Recruitment rules under Central or State Act.- If the posts are created under the Act and the Act provides to make rules for appointment and service conditions of such posts, the following aspects shall be checked:-
  • (i) whether the power is conferred upon the Government under the Act to make recruitment rules for the post;
  • (ii) whether the recruitment rules are as per the provisions of the Act;
  • (iii) whether the recruitment rules are approved by the General Administration Department;
  • (iv) whether the recruitment rules are approved by the Maharashtra Public Service Commission, unless the posts are exempted from the purview of the Maharashtra Public Service Commission under the Maharashtra Public Service Commission (Exemption from Consultation) Regulation, 1965;
  • (v) whether existing rules needs to be superseded ;
  • (vi) other relevant provisions as specified in check list above in paragraph 16.1.for the rules to be made under the proviso to Article 309 of the Constitution. Rules under Central or State Act.
  • 16.3. Rules under Central or State Act.-The following shall be checked while scrutinizing the draft rules to be made under the provisions of State or Central Act,-
  • (i) whether the power is conferred upon the State Government under the Act to make rules;
  • (ii) whether the previous publication of rules is required;
  • (iii) whether the rules proposed to make with the power to dispense with the condition of previous publication; and whether the power to dispense with is provided in the Act;
  • (iv) whether existing rules, if any, needs to be superseded;
  • (v) whether the consequential amendments are required to be made in other rules;
  • (vi) whether the proposed rules are in consonance with the provisions of the Parent Act;
  • (vii) whether the consultation with any authority is required as per the provisions of the Act;
  • (viii) whether any further delegation is proposed in the rules;
  • (ix) if the rules are proposed to be made with retrospective effect, whether power to make rules with retrospective effect is conferred under the Act;
  • (x) in case of final publication of rules, whether any substantial changes has made by the Department which were not in the preliminary draft published for suggestions and objections.

H 2316-18a

GUIDELINES FOR DRAFTING OF SUBORDINATE LEGISLATION

  • 17. The following are the general guidelines for drafting/preparing of Subordinate Legislation (Notifications, Rules, Regulations, By-laws or any instrument). Enabling power.

17.1

It shall be made only if there is enabling power in the Act. Power conferred.

  • 17.2 It shall be issued under the sections under which the power is conferred. Preamble. 17.3 Precise statutory authority should be cited to enable one to know whether the subordinate legislation has been framed under due legal authority and within the limits laid down in the Parent Act. Subject of subordinate legislation.

17.4

It shall be made for the subject matter to which the power is conferred by the Act. Power to whom conferred.

17.5

It shall be made by the Government or Government authority to which the power is conferred by the Act. Citation of enactments.

  • 17.6 Section number /sub-section/clause under which the power is conferred and the name of the Act (short title) and number with year of Act, shall be cited in the preamble of such rules or bye-laws, notification, instrument or document. By order and in name of the Governor.
  • 17.7 (1) Any instrument made or issued on behalf of the State Government, shall be expressed to be made in the name of the Governor as provided in Article 166(1) of the Constitution.
  • (2) At the end of the instrument and above the name of the officer who will sign the instrument, the following expression shall be printed:- “By order and in the name of the Governor of Maharashtra”. Name. 17.8 The name of the officer who will sign the instrument shall be mentioned at the end of such instrument. Not contrary to Act.
  • 17.9 It shall not override the provisions of the Parent Act or shall not inconsistent with the provisions of the Act. Conformity with Constitution.
  • 17.10 It shall not violate the provisions of the Constitution. The rulemaking power is subject to the Constitutional provisions which limit the powers of the State Legislature.

Similar manner and conditions.

  • 17.11 Where a power to issue rules is conferred, then that power includes a power, “exercisable in the like manner and subject to the like sanctions and conditions, if any, to add, to amend, vary or rescind any rule so issued (see section 21 of the General Clauses Act, 1897 and the Maharashtra General Clauses Act (I of 1904). Therefore, subordinate legislation should be amended in the like manner in which it was made.

Sub delegation.

  • 17.12 It shall be made by the authority or body to whom the power is conferred by the Act. The authority to whom the power is conferred cannot further delegate such power to the authority/officer subordinate to it, unless, it is authorized by the Act.

Definitions. 17.13 While framing rules under the Act, the definitions used in the Act need not to define again in rules. New definitions may be incorporated in rules as per requirements, but subject to provisions of Parent Act.

No repeat provisions of Act.

  • 17.14 The provisions which have been made in the Act, the same provision should not be repeated in the subordinate legislation.

Use same words.

  • 17.15 Words/terms used in Act, the same words/terms should be used in the subordinate legislation.

For same purpose.

  • 17.16 It shall be framed for the purposes for which the power is delegated in the Act.

Consultation. 17.17 If the Act provides that the rules, regulations or by-laws shall be made in consultation or approval or sanction or permission of the authorities or bodies; such rules, regulations or by-laws shall be made in consultation or approval or sanction or permission of that authority or bodies.

Previous publication.

  • 17.18 If the Act provides the rules shall be made after previous publication, the rules shall be published in the Official Gazette, for inviting suggestions and objections. The sufficient time limit for submitting suggestions and objections shall be provided in draft rules. As per the recommendations of Committee on Subordinate Legislation of Parliament, a period of not less than thirty clear days should invariably be given to the public to send their comments/suggestions on draft rules.

After considering such suggestions and objections, the final rules shall be published in the Official Gazette, after approval from the Law and Judiciary Department.

Publication in Gazette.

  • 17.19 (1) Publication is essential for the validity of subordinate legislation. Rules or regulations are binding on the people only after their publication in the Official Gazette. (Harla Vs. State of Rajasthan, AIR 1951 SC 467).
  • (2) If the Act provides the Notification / Order shall be published in the Official Gazette, the Notification /Order shall be published in the Official Gazette. If the Act is silent on publication, in such case, it is not necessary to publish the notification/order in the Official Gazette. However, it can be published in the Official Gazette, for the information of the general public. If it is published in the Official Gazette, it will get the authenticity. Publication of the amending notifications, orders, etc.
  • 17. 20 The amending notifications/orders/rules/regulations/bye-laws, etc. should be published in the same part of the Gazette in which the principal notification/order/rules/regulations/byelaws had been published.

Year of publication.

  • 17.21 The year of publication and the year in short title should be the same.

Number of amendments.

  • 17.22 If more than one amendment is carried out in a particular year then, the amendment number should be given in reference to that year only.

Commenceme nt.

  • 17.23 (1) The notification and rules will come into force on the date of their publication in the Official Gazette, unless different date is provided in the notification and rules.
  • (2) If the Department intends to bring into force the rules or notification on particular day it shall provide the date in such rules or notification.
  • (3) The notification and rules can’t give retrospective effect unless specifically empowered by the Act. Laying. 17.24 If the Act provides the rules or notifications shall be laid before each House of the State Legislature, the same shall be laid before each House of the State Legislature. The amending rules shall also to be laid before each House of the State Legislature.

Retrospective provisions.

  • 17.25 Unless the Parent or enabling Act specifically gives power to make Rules or notification with retrospective effect, no retrospective effect shall be given to such rules or notification. Repeal or superseded or rescind.
  • 17.26 If the Notifications, Rules, Regulations or By-laws to be amended or repealed or superseded or rescinded, it shall be amended or repealed or superseded or rescinded, in exercises of the same power under which they have been issued or made. Subsequent amendments.
  • 17.27 If amendments to be made in any provisions of rules/notifications which has been subsequently added by amendments in such cases, the amendments shall be made in principal/orginals notification rules and not in the amending notification/rules. Rules under Central Act.
  • 17.28 If Notification/ Rules to be issued under Central Act, in the preamble of Notification/Rules, after the name of Act, the words “in its application to the State of Maharashtra” shall be mentioned. Reference of General Clauses Act.
  • 17.29 While rescinding the notification / order, it shall be issued by exercising the same powers under which the notification / order was issued; the reference of “section 21 of the Maharashtra General Clauses Act (I of 1904)” shall also to be made in preamble of the notification/order. Amendment number.
  • 17.30 If the Rules, Regulations, By-laws are amended for the first time in the year, it shall be mentioned as “Amendment” and if it is amended for second or more time, in that year, it shall be mentioned as “Second Amendment” “Third Amendment”, etc. Earlier reference.
  • 17.31 If the existing Notifications, Rules, Regulations or By-laws are to be repealed and new Notifications, Rules, Regulations or By-laws are to be made ; in such cases, in new draft, the reference of supersession of earlier Notifications, Rules, Regulations or By-laws shall be made. Not override provisions of the principal Act.

17.32

The Subordinate Legislation should not override the provisions of the principal Act under which the Subordinate Legislation is made. Amendments. 17.33 If the amendments to be made in the principal Rules or in notification or order, then it shall be made by exercising the same power under which the principal Rules or notification or order have been made or issued.

Plain language.

  • 17.34 As far as possible, the plain English should be used as a language in drafting the subordinate legislation.

Rule making power.

  • 17.35 (1) The rules are framed by the State Government and the regulations and bye-laws are framed by the authorities or institutions subordinate to the Government, to whom the power is confirmed by the Act.
  • (2) When rule making power is conferred by the Act in general terms “for the purposes of carrying out the provisions of the Act”, the purposes of the Act must be determined with reference to all the provisions of the Act read together.
  • (3) Where a specific power is conferred without prejudice to the generality of the general power already specified, the particular power is only illustrative and does not, in any way, restrict the general powers.
  • (4) The rules shall be made for the purposes for which the power is conferred by the Act. ______________________________________________ Note:- The some contents of this Chapter are taken from the Books of Legislative DraftingB.E. Atre, Legislative Drafting-Shaping the Law for the New Millennium-T.K.Viswanathan, Principles of Legislation and Legislative draftingN.K.Chakrabarti.

H 2316-19

CHAPTER XVIII

GUIDELINES FOR DELEGATED LEGISLATION

Guidelines for Delegated Legislation. 18 The Rules, Regulations and Bye-laws are made and the notifications and orders are issued under the Act, for which the power is delegated to the Government and subordinate authorities thereto. In Delhi Laws case, reported in A.I.R. 38, 1951 S.C. at pages 332 to 344, the Supreme Court has laid down certain legal principles for delegated legislation.

  • 18.1 The essential legislative functions shall not be delegated.
  • 18.2 The following are the matters which should be embodied in the Act itself constituting as they are the essential legislative functions:-
  • (1) Declaring what the laws shall be in relation to any particular territory or locality.
  • (2) Extending the duration or operation of an Act.
  • (3) Repealing or amending the law.
  • (4) Modifying any existing or future law in any essential feature so as to involve a change of policy.
  • (5) Power to levy tax.
  • (6) Power to levy fees.
  • (7) Power to prescribe punishment. The making of a particular act into an offence and prescribing punishment for it is an essential legislative function and cannot be delegated by the legislature to the executive. Note.-It is permissible to prescribe penalties by rules provided the Parent Act contains a specific provision to this effect and further the maximum amount of penalty is also laid down in the Act. If the legislature lays down the standards or principles to be followed by the executive in defining an offence and provides the limits of penalties, such delegation is permissible. There are two important safeguards, if provided, there may not be any objection to prescribe the punishments through the delegated legislation. They are :-

H 2316-19a

  • (i) the legislature must determine the maximum punishment which the rule-making authority may prescribe; and
  • (ii) if such power is delegated to any authority other than the State, the exercise of the power must be subject to the previous sanction.
  • (8) Power to allow exemptions. Note.- After laying down the policy of the law and the standards to be applied in its administration, the Legislature may authorize the Executive to make rules or regulations prescribing classes of cases in which relief may be granted.
  • (9) Expenditure from Consolidated Fund, as for instance, appointment of employees or staff, payment of fees and travelling allowances to them. Note:- Rules may govern these matters provided the Act contains the necessary enabling provision and adequate indication is given in the Act that expenditure from the public revenues is likely to be incurred on these matters.
  • (10) Matters affecting the jurisdiction of the Courts.
  • (11) Right of appeal. Note.- When the entire machinery for taking original decisions is created under rules authorised by the enabling provisions of the Act, there may be no objection to provide also by rules, machinery and procedure for appeals against such decisions; but if the original decisions are taken under the specific provisions of the Act and there is no provisions of appeal made in the Act, it will not be right to fill the lacuna by making provisions regarding appeals under the rules on grounds of natural justice.
  • (12) Provisions affecting interest or rights in properties and providing for compensation.
  • (13) Trespassing upon individual rights and liberties. Note.- (For clauses (12) and (13)).- Rules may be made affecting interests or rights in properties and providing for compensation therefor or for the matter of that, trespassing upon individual rights and liberties, provided there are specific enabling provisions in the Act and sufficient guidelines are indicated therein regarding the manner in which and the extent to which those rights, interests and liberties are going to be affected.
  • (14) Removal of difficulties: (a) The enabling provision should be included in Act. When the legislature passes an Act, it cannot foresee all the difficulties which may arise in implementing it. The executive is, therefore, empowered to make necessary changes to remove such difficulties. This provision has been nicknamed as Henri VIII clause.
  • (b) The power to remove difficulties may also be delegated subject to a time-limit of two years for its operation from the passing of the Act.
  • 18.3 Matters may be permitted in Rules.-The following matters may be permitted to be governed by the Rules:-
  • (1) All matters of subsidiary or ancillary nature or those which relate to procedure or matters of detail.
  • (2) Fees can be prescribed if there is enabling provision in the Act.
  • (3) As regards taxation, it seems permissible to delegate the power to carry cut a certain taxation policy if the principles of taxation are clearly embodied in the Act; examples of these may be mentioned below:-
  • (a) Powers to determine the time when or the manner in which tax should be paid, that is, power to determine matters of computation, appraisement, adjustment and such other like powers involving more certainty of details.
  • (b) Power to authorise administrative authority to mathematically reduce the rate from facts and events referred to in the taxing measure.
  • (c) Power to revise rates according to the changing circumstances, provided definite standards are laid down to guide the exercise of such powers.
  • (d) Rate-making function provided adequate standards are provided to guide the administrative body in rate-making process.
  • (e) In matter of sales tax, power to prescribe by rules at what single point in the series of sales by successive dealers the goods shall be liable to tax.
  • (f) Discretion as regards the procedure to be followed in the matter of collection or assessment of taxes, provided sufficient guide or standard for the exercise of such discretion is provided in the law.
  • (g) Power to determine whether a particular article or merchandise is suitable, after the Legislature had laid down the principles and the rate according to which a duty is to be levied.
  • (4) Penalties can be prescribe by rules provided that the Parent Act contains a specific provision to this effect and further the maximum amount of penalty is also laid down in the Act.
  • (5) Exemptions can be prescribed by rules if after laying down the policy of the law and the standards to be applied in its administration, the Legislature may authorize the Executive to make rules or regulations prescribing classes of cases in which exemption can be granted.
  • 18.4 Retrospective effect to rules.- The following principles shall be followed while giving retrospective effect to the rules:-
  • (1) power to give retrospective effect must flow from the Act itself either by express words or by necessary intendment, which must be gathered from the provisions of the Act itself;
  • (2) retrospective effect should be given only to confer benefits on the subjects and not to impose any new obligations or restrictions affecting the existing rights, and that too in circumstances which are compelling and the power should be used sparingly;
  • (3) normally, the date of retrospective operation should not be earlier than the date when the Act comes into force unless the Act specifically provides otherwise. Reference.- Subordinate Legislation Committee Report.

GOVERNMENT RESOLUTION

Government Resolution.

19. Rule 110 of the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly Rules

provides the procedure for passing Government Resolution in the Maharashtra Legislative Assembly.

  • 110. Government Resolutions. (1) The provisions of rule 106 shall not apply to resolutions of which notice is given by a Minister or the Advocate General.
  • (2) Seven days’ notice shall be necessary in respect of such resolutions.

RATIFICATION OF AMENDMENT TO CONSTITUTION

Constitution provisions.

  • 19.1. Constitational provisions for making amendments in the Constitution.

“368. Power of Parliament to amend the Constitution and

procedure therefor.

  • (1) Notwithstanding anything in this Constitution, Parliament may in exercise of its constituent power amend by way of addition, variation or repeal any provision of this Constitution in accordance with the procedure laid down in this article.
  • (2) An amendment of this Constitution may be initiated only by the introduction of a Bill for the purpose in either House of Parliament, and when the Bill is passed in each House by a majority of the total membership of that House and by a majority of not less than two-thirds of the members of that House present and voting, it shall be presented to the President who shall give his assent to the Bill and thereupon the Constitution shall stand amended in accordance with the terms of the Bill: Provided that if such amendment seeks to make any change in—
  • (a) article 54, article 55, article 73, article 162, article 241 or article 279A or
  • (b) Chapter IV of Part V, Chapter V of Part VI, or Chapter I of Part XI, or
  • (c) any of the Lists in the Seventh Schedule, or
  • (d) the representation of States in Parliament, or
  • (e) the provisions of this article, the amendment shall also require to be ratified by the Legislatures of not less than one-half of the States by resolutions to that effect passed by those Legislatures before the Bill making provision for such amendment is presented to the President for assent.”. Maharashtra Legislative Assembly Rules provisions.
  • 19.2. The following procedure is adopted in State Legislature for ratification of amendment to the Constitution of India. Rules 160 to 162 of the Assembly Rules and rules 155 to 157 of Council Rules provides the procedure for ratification of amendment to the Constitution. Ratification of amendment to the Constitution.

“160. (1) As soon as a message for ratification of the amendment to the Constitution is received from a House of Parliament, a copy of the message together with the Bill, shall be laid by the Secretary on the Table of the House.

  • (2) The Secretary shall forward a copy of the message together with the Bill to the State Government at the first convenient opportunity.

Discussion on the amendment to the Constitution.

  • 161. (1) After the message has been laid on the Table under Rule 160, any member may, after giving three days’ notice, move a resolution that the amendment to the Constitution proposed by the Bill be ratified.
  • (2) The Speaker shall, in consultation with the Leader of the House, fix a date for discussion on the resolution.
  • (3) No amendment shall be proposed to such a resolution.
  • (4) The Speaker may, if he thinks fit, prescribe a time limit for speeches.

Message to Parliament.

  • 162. (1) If the resolution is passed by the House, a message shall be send to the House of Parliament from which the message referred to in rule 160 was received, that the Assembly ratifies the amendment.
  • (2) If the resolution is rejected by the House, a message shall be send as aforesaid that the Assembly does not ratify the amendment.
  • (3) If no resolution is moved during the Session in which the message of the House of Parliament is laid on the Table, the Secretary shall send information to that effect to the House of parliament from which the message was received.”.

19.3 Procedure of Government Resolution.

  • (1) The concerned Administrative Department shall prepare the draft resolution (English, Marathi) and shall send to the Law and Judiciary Department for scrutiny.
  • (2) The Law and Judiciary Department will make scrutiny of draft Resolution and will send to the concerned Department.
  • (3) The concerned Administrative Department shall take the Cabinet approval for the Government Resolution.
  • (4) The concerned Minister of the Department will give Notice of Resolution to the Maharashtra State Legislature alongwith the Resolution.
  • (5) If the Resolution is relating to ratification of Constitution Amendment, after passing of such Resolution, it will be sent by the Mahaarashtra State Legislature to the House of Parliament from which the message was received.
  • (6) If the resolution is passed by the House, a message will be send to the House of Parliament from which the message was received, that the Assembly and Council has ratified the amendment.

ANNEXURE-I STANDARD FORMATS OF DRAFTING (AMENDMENTS)

The amendments are made for different purposes and by different ways. The amendments are made to delete, to add, to substitute the words, portion, section, sub-section, clause, paragraph, entry, etc., of the Act. The amendments may be made with retrospective effect or prospective effect, it depends on the contingencies arises. The purpose of the amending Act is to plant the necessary amendments in the Principal Act/Parent Act/Main Act, and once such amendment has been effected, the Amending Act has served its purpose, the amendments take root in the Main Act. The following are the drafting formula/style may be adopted while drafting.

H 2316-22

ANNEXURE-II STANDARD FORMAT OF DRAFTING (Bill)

  • (1) Long title.
  • (a) Principal Legislation Long title. The Maharashtra International Sports University Act, 2020.

A Bill

to establish and incorporate an International Sports University, MAHARASHTRA in the State of Maharashtra, a specialized University first of its kind, to create competent personnel in sports, to promote physical education and sports education in the areas of sports sciences, sports medicine, sports technology, sports governing, sports management, sports media and communication, sports coaching and training, besides functioning as the State training center for select sports disciplines by adopting best international practices and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto.

Mah. XXXV of 2020.

The Maharashtra Witness Protection and Security Act, 2017.

A Bill

to provide protection to the witnesses in criminal trials, their relatives in relation to serious offences and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto. Mah. IX of 2018.

  • (b) Amending Legislation

A Bill

further to amend the Maharashtra Agricultural Universities (Krishi Vidyapeeths) Act, 1983 and the Maharashtra Animal and Fishery Sciences University Act, 1998. Mah. XLI of 1983.

Mah. XVII of 1998.

A Bill

further to amend certain tax laws in operation in the State of Maharashtra.

  • (c) Amendments in Central Act

A Bill

further to amend the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970, in its application to the State of Maharashtra.

37 of 1970.

Preamble. (2) Preamble.

  • (a) Principal Act WHEREAS the practice of collecting capitation fee for admitting students into educational institutions and at the time of promoting students to a higher standard or class at various stages of education is on the increase in the State; AND WHEREAS this undesirable practice has been contributing to large scale commercialization of education which is not conducive to the maintenance of educational standards; AND WHEREAS the National Policy on Education 1986 envisages that the commercialization of technical and professional education should be curbed and that steps should be taken to prevent the establishment of institutions set up to commercialize education; AND WHEREAS with a view to effectively curb this evil practice, it is expedient in the public interest to prohibit collection of capitation fee for admission of students to, and their promotion to a higher standard or class in, the educational institutions in the State of Maharashtra and to provide for matters connected therewith; It is hereby enacted in the Thirty-eighth Year of the Republic of India as follows :—

Principal Act

WHEREAS it is expedient to make special provisions for payment of compensation for loss of life of, or injury to, humans and cattle and damage to crops and property caused by certain wild animals and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto; it is hereby enacted in the Seventy-fourth Year of the Republic of India, as follows:— H 2316-22a

  • (b) Amending Act WHEREAS it is expedient further to amend the Maharashtra Agricultural Lands (Ceiling on Holdings) Act, 1961, for the purposes hereinafter appearing; it is hereby enacted in the Seventy-fourth Year of the Republic of India, as follows :—
  • (c) Ordinance conversion WHEREAS both Houses of the State Legislature were not in session; AND WHEREAS the Governor of Maharashtra was satisfied that circumstances existed which rendered it necessary for him to take immediate action further to amend the Maharashtra Public Universities Act, 2016, for the purposes hereinafter appearing ; and, therefore, promulgated the Maharashtra Public Universities (Amendment) Ordinance, 2023, on the 20th October 2023;
  • (d) Ordinance WHEREAS both Houses of the State Legislature are not in session; AND WHEREAS the Governor of Maharashtra is satisfied that circumstances exist which render it necessary for him to take immediate action further to amend the Maharashtra Public Universities Act, 2016, for the purposes hereinafter appearing; NOW, THEREFORE, in exercise of the powers conferred by clause (1) of article 213 of the Constitution of India, the Governor of Maharashtra is hereby pleased to promulgate the following Ordinance, namely:—
  • (e) Amendments in Central Act WHEREAS it is expedient further to amend the Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition) Act, 1970, in its application to the State of Maharashtra, for the purposes hereinafter appearing; it is hereby enacted in the Sixty-seventh Year of the Republic of India as follows :— 37 of 1970.

Offences by company.

  • (16) Offences by company.

The Maharashtra Water Resources Regulatory Authority

Act, 2005. “27. Offences by company.- (1) Where an offence under this Act has been committed by a company, every person who at the time, when the offence was committed, was in charge of, and was responsible to the company for the conduct of the business of the company as well as the company, shall be deemed to be guilty of the offence and shall be liable to be proceeded against and punished accordingly : Provided that, nothing contained in this sub-section shall render any such person liable to any punishment under this Act if he proves that the offence was committed without his knowledge or that he had exercised all due diligence to prevent the commission of such offence.

  • (2) Notwithstanding anything contained in sub-section (1), where an offence under this Act has been committed by a company and it is proved that the offence has been committed with the consent or connivance of, or is attributable to any neglect on the part of, any director, manager, secretary or other officer of the company, such director, manager, secretary or other officer shall also be deemed to be guilty of that offence and shall be liable to be proceeded against and punished accordingly.”.

Mah.XVI II of 2005.

Compoundin g of offences.

  • (17) Compounding of offences.

The Maharashtra Water Resources Regulatory Authority Act, 2005,-

“28. Compounding of offences.

  • (1) The Authority may, either before or after the institution of the proceedings for any offence Mah. XVIII of 2005.

H 2316-23a punishable under this Act with the approval of the State Government accept from any person charged with such offence, by way of composition of the offence, a sum not less than the amount of the maximum fine and not more than double the amount of the maximum fine for the offences punishable by or under this Act.

  • (2) On payment of such sum as may be determined by the Authority or the State Government or any officer authorised by the State Government under sub-section (1), no further proceedings shall be taken against the accused person in respect of the same offence, and any proceedings if already taken or initiated shall stand abated, and the accused person, if in custody, shall be discharged.”. Act, 2006. Penalties. 9. (1) Whoever contravenes any of the provisions of this Act or any rules, notification or order made under this Act shall, on conviction, be punished,—

Repealed and saving.

  • 58. Repeal and saving. (1) On the commencement of this Act, the following laws, that is to say,—
  • (a) the Bombay Rents, Hotel and Lodging House Rates Control Act, 1947;

Mah. XVIII of 2000.

  • (b) the Central Provinces and Berar Regulation of Letting of Accommodation Act, 1946 including the Central Provinces and Berar Letting of Houses and Rent Control Order, 1949; and

  • (2) Notwithstanding such repeal,—
  • (a) all applications, suits and other proceedings under the said Acts, pending on the date of commencement of this Act before any Court, Controller, Competent Authority or other office or authority, shall be continued and disposed of, in accordance with the provisions of the Acts so repealed, as if the said Acts had continued in force and this Act had not been passed ;
  • (b) the provisions for appeal under the Acts so repealed shall continue in force in respect of applications, suits and proceedings disposed of thereunder ;
  • (c) any appointment, rule and notification made or issued under any of the repealed Acts and in force on the date of commencement of this Act shall, in so far as they are not inconsistent with the provisions of this Act, be deemed to have been made or issued under this Act and shall continue in force until it is superseded or modified by any appointment, rule or notification made or issued under this Act;
  • (d) all prosecutions instituted under the provisions of any of the repealed Acts shall be effective and disposed of in accordance with the law. Review. (33) Review. The Maharashtra Settlement of Arrears of Tax, Interest, Penalty or Late Fee Act, 2022.

Validation. (39) Validation.

The Maharashtra Stamp (Amendment and Validation)

Act, 2021.

  • 4. (1) Notwithstanding anything contained in any judgment, decree or order of any court to the contrary Mah. III of 2021.

or in the principal Act, stamp duty assessed, levied and collected, including any action taken in pursuance of such assessment, levy and collection by the authorities under the said Act, acting or purporting to act under the provisions of section 5 and articles 6 and 40 in the Schedule I to the principal Act shall be deemed to have been validly levied and collected in accordance with law as if the provisions of the said section 5 and articles 6 and 40, as amended by the Maharashtra Stamp (Amendment and Validation) Act, 2021 (hereinafter in this section referred to as “the Amendment Act”) had been continuously in force at all material time and accordingly,—

  • (a) all actions, proceedings or things done or taken by the authorities under the principal Act in connection with the levy and collection of the stamp duty shall for all purposes, be deemed to have been done or taken in accordance with the provisions of the principal Act ;
  • (b) no suit or other proceedings shall be maintainable or continued in any court, against the said authorities for the refund of the stamp duty so levied and collected ;
  • (c) no court or any other authority shall enforce any decree or order directing the refund of the stamp duty so levied or collected.
  • (2) For the removal of doubts, it is hereby declared that nothing in sub-section (1) shall be construed as preventing a person,—
  • (a) from questioning in accordance with the provisions of the principal Act as amended by the Amendment Act, any assessment, reassessment, levy or collection of stamp duty referred to in sub-section (1), or
  • (b) from claiming refund of any stamp duty paid by him under the principal Act, in excess of the amount due from him by way of stamp duty under the principal Act, as amended by the Amendment Act.

Validation and savings.

  • (40) Validating and savings.
  • 11. Validating and saving.- Notwithstanding anything contained in the Maharashtra Slum Areas (Improvement, Clearance and Redevelopment) Act, 1971 or any judgement, decree or order of any court, tribunal or authority to the contrary, all acts, proceedings or things done or taken, including the orders passed by the Apex Grievance Redressal Committee and Grievance Redressal Committees, during the period commencing from 8th March 2017 and ending on the date of commencement of the Maharashtra Slum Areas (Improvement, Clearance and Redevelopment) (Amendment, Re-enactment of Rules and Notification of Apex and other Grievance Redressal Committees and Validation) Act, 2023 (hereinafter, in this section, referred to as “the Amendment Act”), under the said Act, the Rules and the Notification, shall be deemed to be and shall be deemed always to have been, duly and validly taken or done in accordance with the law as if the provisions of the said Act, as amended by the Amendment Act, had been continuously in force at all material times and accordingly, all actions taken or proceedings or things done by any officer or authority in connection with any Slum Rehabilitation Scheme, in pursuance of the orders passed by the said Committees, shall for all the purposes, be deemed to be and shall be deemed always to have been done or taken in accordance with the provisions of the said Act, as amended by the Amendment Act.
